1<\beta^{\prime}\leq\beta
.

Obviously,
\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})
. Since any
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})
satisfies
\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}\leq H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}
, it holds that
\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}\|_{2}\leq\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}+\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}\leq H_{q,k}
, which implies that
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{B}(\widetilde{\bm{x}},H_{q,k})
. Therefore,
\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})\cap\mathcal{B}(\widetilde{\bm{x}},H_{q,k})
. We can bound
\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})]
by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})]

\displaystyle=(\frac{H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}}{H_{q,k}})^{d}\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})]

\displaystyle=(1-\frac{\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}}{H_{q,k}})^{d}\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})]

\displaystyle\geq(1-\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}})\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})].

(A.20)

where the last inequality is by (
A.2.4
).

Assumption
6
gives that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{R}_{k}\cap\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})]\geq\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d-1}}\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})],

which implies that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})\setminus(\mathcal{R}_{k}\cap\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}))]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d-1}})\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d-1}})\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})].

Therefore, together with (
A.2.4
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{R}_{k}\cap\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}))]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})]-(1-\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d-1}})\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}}-1+\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d-1}})\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}}\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})].

Recall that
\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})\cap\mathcal{B}(\widetilde{\bm{x}},H_{q,k})
and
\mathcal{R}_{k}\subseteq S_{q,k}
, then

\displaystyle\operatorname{Leb}[S_{q,k}\cap\mathcal{B}(\widetilde{\bm{x}},H_{q,k})]

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{R}_{k}\cap\mathcal{B}(\widetilde{\bm{x}},H_{q,k})]

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{R}_{k}\cap\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k}-\|\widetilde{\bm{x}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2})]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}}\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{0},H_{q,k})]

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}}\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{B}(\widetilde{\bm{x}},H_{q,k})],

which gives that
S_{q,k}
is weakly
(\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}},H_{q,k})
-regular at
\widetilde{\bm{x}}
. This contradicts the assumption, thus implying that for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
,
S_{q,k}
is weakly
(\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}},H_{q,k})
-regular at every
\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}\cap G
. This finishes the proof.
\square

A.2.5
Proof of Proposition
3.7

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{q}=\left\{\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}}N_{q,k}\geq\left(\frac{4^{q}\log(T\delta_{A}^{-d})}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{q}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\text{(i) }\text{ for all }k\in\mathcal{K},S_{q,k}\text{ is weakly }(\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}},H_{q,k})\text{-regular at all }\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}\cap G\\
&\text{(ii) }\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|\leq\epsilon_{q}/2\text{ for all }\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}\text{ and }k\in\mathcal{K}\end{aligned}\right\}.

We first show the following two inequalities:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{G}_{q}^{C}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q}\right)\leq\frac{\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)K}{T},

(A.21)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{M}_{q}^{C}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)\leq\frac{K}{T}.

(A.22)

Proof of (
A.21
)
.
When
T
is sufficiently large, we have that under event
\mathcal{M}_{q}
,
\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}}N_{q,k}\geq\left(\frac{4^{q}\log(T\delta_{A}^{-d})}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\geq\left(\frac{6\sqrt{M_{\beta}}Lv_{d}p_{\max}}{p^{*}\lambda_{0}\epsilon_{q}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta}}
by direct computation. By Proposition
3.6
, under event
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q}
, for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
,
S_{q,k}
is weakly
(\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}},H_{q,k})
-regular at all
\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}\cap G
, which proves (i) of event
\mathcal{G}_{q}
. Then the conditions in Lemma
A.8
are satisfied. We finish the proof by showing that

\displaystyle K\delta_{A}^{-d}\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K\delta_{A}^{-d}\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-4^{q}\log(T\delta_{A}^{-d})\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}4^{-q}(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)K}{T}.

Proof of (
A.22
)
. Lemma
A.5
implies that
\mathcal{R}_{k}\subseteq S_{q,k}
for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
. Together with Assumptions
4
and
6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(N_{q,k}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{I}\left\{\pi_{t}=k\right\}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{P}\left(\pi_{t}=k\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{P}\left(\pi_{t}=k,\bm{x}_{t}\in S_{q,k}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{K}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{R}_{k}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)\geq\frac{p^{*}}{K}|\mathcal{T}_{q}|.

When
q>1
, the decisions in the epoch
q
are only dependent on the history samples, thus it is obvious that
{\mathcal{T}}_{q,k}
are i.i.d. conditional on
\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}\}_{h=1}^{q-1}
. Together with Hoeffding’s inequality,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(N_{q,k}<\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\bigg|\penalty\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1},\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}\}_{h=1}^{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\left(N_{q,k}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{k-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{k-1}\right)-N_{q,k}>\frac{p^{*}}{K}|\mathcal{T}_{q}|

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\bigg|\penalty\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1},\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}\}_{h=1}^{q-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{2}{|\mathcal{T}_{q}|}\left[\frac{p^{*}}{K}|\mathcal{T}_{q}|-\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{2}{|\mathcal{T}_{q}|}\left(\frac{p^{*2}}{K^{2}}|\mathcal{T}_{q}|^{2}-\frac{2p^{*}}{K}|\mathcal{T}_{q}|\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{2p^{*2}}{K^{2}}|\mathcal{T}_{q}|+\frac{4p^{*}}{K}\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\right).

Since

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}+\frac{K^{2}}{2p^{*2}}\log T\right\rceil

\displaystyle>\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}+\frac{K^{2}}{2p^{*2}}\log T,

we have

\displaystyle\frac{2p^{*2}}{K^{2}}|\mathcal{T}_{q}|-\frac{4p^{*}}{K}\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\geq\log T,

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(N_{q,k}<\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\bigg|\penalty\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1},\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}\}_{h=1}^{q-1}\right)\leq\frac{1}{T}.

In that way, when marginalizing over
\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}\}_{h=1}^{q-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}}N_{q,k}<\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\bigg|\penalty\ \overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)\leq\frac{K}{T}.

Proof of (
3.4
) in Proposition
3.7
. It can be verified that

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q}^{C}=\cup_{j=1}^{q}\mathcal{G}_{j}^{C}

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}(\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j})\subseteq\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}(\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j})\cup(\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j})

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}(\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j})\cup(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j})

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}\left(\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j})\cup(\bigcup_{i=0}^{j}{\mathcal{M}}_{i+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\right)\cup\left(\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j}\right).

Similarly,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q}^{C}=\cup_{j=1}^{q}\mathcal{M}_{j}^{C}

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}(\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j})\subseteq\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}(\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j})\cup(\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j})

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}(\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j})\cup(\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j})

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}\left((\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j})\cup(\bigcup_{i=0}^{j-1}{\mathcal{G}}_{i+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}(\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j})\cup\left(\bigcup_{j=0}^{q-1}\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\right).

Therefore, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{k}^{C}\cup\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{k}^{C}\subseteq\left(\bigcup_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\right)\cup\left(\bigcup_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j}\right).

With (
A.21
) and (
A.22
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q}^{C}\cup\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q}^{C}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\right)+\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\cap\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{G}_{j+1}^{C}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j+1}\right)+\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{M}_{j+1}^{C}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{j},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}\frac{\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)K}{T}+\frac{K}{T}=\frac{\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)qK}{T}.

This finishes the proof.
\square

A.2.6
Proof of Theorem
3.8

When
t\in\mathcal{T}_{1}
, the algorithm pulls all arms with equal probability, which implies that for all
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}
,
\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=i|\mathcal{F}_{t-1},\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x})=1/K
for all
i\in\mathcal{K}
. Thus, fairness condition is satisfied trivially at
t
.

At epoch
q>1
, when
\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x}
, the algorithm randomly pulls arms from set
\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}
with equal probability. For the pair of arms
i,j\in\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}
,
\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=i|\mathcal{F}_{t-1},\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x})=\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=j|\mathcal{F}_{t-1},\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x})\geq 1/K
. On the other hand, when
i,j\in\mathcal{K}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}
,
\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=i|\mathcal{F}_{t-1},\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x})=\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=j|\mathcal{F}_{t-1},\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x})=0
. The former two cases automatically satisfy fairness constraint (
2.2
). Therefore, it remains to consider the arm pairs with
i\in\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}
and
j\in\mathcal{K}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}
, which satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=i|\mathcal{F}_{t-1},\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x})\geq\frac{1}{K}>\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=j|\mathcal{F}_{t-1},\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x})=0
. For
j\in\mathcal{K}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}
, there exists an epoch
1\leq l\leq q-1
such that
j\in\mathcal{K}_{l,u(\bm{x})}
and
j\in\mathcal{K}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{l+1,u(\bm{x})}
. Moreover, the selection rule implies that
i\in\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{l,u(\bm{x})}
when
l<q
. Thus,
\widehat{f}_{l,j}(\bm{x})
is not
(2\epsilon_{l})
-chained to
\widehat{f}_{l,j}(\bm{x})
in
\{\widehat{f}_{l,k}(\bm{x}):k\in\mathcal{K}_{l,u(\bm{x})}\}
, which gives

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{l,i}\left(\bm{x}\right)-\widehat{f}_{l,j}\left(\bm{x}\right)>2\epsilon_{l}.

Under event
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}
,

\displaystyle f_{i}^{*}\left(\bm{x}\right)-f_{j}^{*}\left(\bm{x}\right)

\displaystyle=f_{i}^{*}\left(\bm{x}\right)-\widehat{f}_{l,i}\left(\bm{x}\right)+\widehat{f}_{l,i}\left(\bm{x}\right)-\widehat{f}_{l,j}\left(\bm{x}\right)+\widehat{f}_{l,j}\left(\bm{x}\right)-f_{j}^{*}\left(\bm{x}\right)

\displaystyle>-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{l}+2\epsilon_{l}-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{l}

\displaystyle>0,

which validates the fairness condition (
2.2
). Thus, under event
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}
, the fairness condition is satisfied for epoch
q
by combining all three cases.

Since the events are nested (i.e.,
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}
implies
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{o}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{o}
for all
o\leq q-1
), it follows that the fairness condition holds for epochs 1 to
q
by induction on earlier epochs. Then under event
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{Q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Q-1}
, the fairness condition is satisfied for all epochs from
1
to
Q
.

According to Proposition
3.7
,
\mathbb{P}\left(\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{Q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Q-1}\right)\geq 1-\frac{K(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}(Q-1))}{T}
. Hence, the fairness condition is satisfied with probability at least
1-\frac{K(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}(Q-1))}{T}\geq 1-\widetilde{O}(1/T)
since Lemma
A.6
gives
Q=O(\log T)
.

\square

A.2.7
Proof of Theorem
3.9

Without loss of generality, we assume
\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. We decompose the expected cumulative regret into two components:

\displaystyle R_{T}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{C}\cup\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{C}\right)\mathbb{P}(\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{C}\cup\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{C})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)+2\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{P}(\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{C}\cup\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{C}),

(A.23)

where the first inequality is because
\mathbb{P}(\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1})\leq 1
, and the last inequality is because
\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
.

By the selection rule, the sample support forms a nested sequence, ensuring
S_{q,k}\subseteq S_{q-1,k}
. Then for
\bm{x}_{t}\in S_{q,\pi_{t}}\subseteq S_{q-1,\pi_{t}}
, we denote
i=\arg\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)
, and have under event
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}
,

\displaystyle f_{i}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq f_{i}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-\widehat{f}_{q-1,i}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)+\widehat{f}_{q-1,i}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-\widehat{f}_{q-1,\pi_{t}}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)+\widehat{f}_{q-1,\pi_{t}}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q-1}+2(K-1)\epsilon_{q-1}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2K\epsilon_{q-1},

(A.24)

where the second inequality is by Lemma
A.5
. Let
A_{t}=\{\bm{x}_{t}:0<\max_{k}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)<2K\epsilon_{q-1}\}
and
B_{t}=\{\bm{x}_{t}:\max_{k}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)=0\}
. By (
A.2.7
), under event
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}
,
\bm{x}_{t}\in A_{t}\cup B_{t}
. Thus, the first term of regret in (
A.2.7
) can be further decomposed into

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{q}}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1},\bm{x}_{t}\in A_{t}\right)\mathbb{P}(\bm{x}_{t}\in A_{t}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{q}}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1},\bm{x}_{t}\in B_{t}\right)\mathbb{P}(\bm{x}_{t}\in B_{t}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{q}}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{\pi_{t}}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1},\bm{x}_{t}\in A_{t}\right)\mathbb{P}(\bm{x}_{t}\in A_{t}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{q}}2K\epsilon_{q-1}\mathbb{P}\left(\exists k,j\mbox{ such that }0<\left|f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{j}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\right|\leq 2K\epsilon_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{q}}2K\epsilon_{q-1}K^{2}(2K\epsilon_{q-1})^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|\epsilon_{q-1}^{\alpha+1}=\widetilde{O}(T^{\frac{\beta+d-\alpha\beta}{2\beta+d}}),

(A.25)

where the second inequality is by margin condition (Assumption
7
), and the last inequality follows from direct computation.

By Theorem
3.7
, the second component can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\mathbb{P}(\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{C}\cup\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{C})\lesssim\sum_{q=1}^{Q}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}}\frac{(q-1)}{T}=\widetilde{O}(1).

(A.26)

Plugging (
A.2.7
) and (
A.26
) into (
A.2.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle R_{T}=\widetilde{O}(T^{\frac{\beta+d-\alpha\beta}{2\beta+d}}).

This convergence rate matches the optimal rate shown in Proposition
A.9
.
Thus, the modified algorithm achieves the minimax-optimal regret rate when
\alpha\beta\leq d
.
\square

A.3
Proofs in Section
4

A.3.1
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Since we have assumed that
\operatorname{Leb}(\mathcal{R}_{i}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j})>c
, there exists a region
D\subset\mathcal{R}_{i}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j}
satisfying
\operatorname{Leb}(D)>c^{\prime}>0
.
The adversary can construct a smooth, perturbed reward function
\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}
for arm
i
such that:

•

\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}
satisfies the same smoothness assumptions as the original function
f_{i}^{*}
.

•

There exists a region
D^{\prime}\subset D
with
\operatorname{Leb}(D^{\prime})>c^{\prime\prime}>0
where
\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x})
is significantly lower than
f_{i}^{*}(\bm{x})
, specifically:

\displaystyle f_{i}^{*}(\bm{x})-\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x})\geq 1\quad\text{for all }\bm{x}\in D^{\prime}.

(A.27)

•

Outside of
D
,
\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x})=f_{i}^{*}(\bm{x})
.

Such a function
\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}
can be easily constructed. Let the adversary set the corruption
c_{t}
as
c_{t}=\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x}_{t})-f_{i}^{*}(\bm{x}_{t})
if the played arm is
\pi_{t}=i
and
t\leq t_{0}
. Consequently, the algorithm observes a corrupted reward:

\displaystyle\widetilde{y}_{t}=f_{i}^{*}(\bm{x}_{t})+c_{t}+\varepsilon_{i,t}=\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x}_{t})+\varepsilon_{i,t},\quad\text{for }\pi_{t}=i,t\leq t_{0}.

Thus, during the initial
t_{0}
rounds, the algorithm effectively perceives
\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}
as the reward function for arm
i
. Let
t_{0}=|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|=\left\lceil\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{4\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}+\frac{K^{2}}{2p^{*2}}\log T\right\rceil
. Then the budget is
C\leq|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|(\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}2\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}+1)\lesssim(\log T)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}=\widetilde{O}(1)
.

The estimators trained by samples collected from epoch 1 exhibit inherently good properties guaranteed by Assumption
4
. Following the same steps in bounding the tail probability of event
\mathcal{G}_{1}
in Proposition
3.7
, we have with probability at least
1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}
, for any
\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{1,j}(\bm{x}_{t})-\widehat{f}_{1,i}(\bm{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=(\widehat{f}_{1,j}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x}_{t}))+f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x}_{t})-\widetilde{f}^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})-(\widehat{f}_{1,i}(\bm{x}_{t})-\widetilde{f}^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t}))

\displaystyle\geq-1/2\epsilon_{1}+1-1/2\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\geq 2\epsilon_{1},

where the first inequality is because samples are collected from the masked function
\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x})
during epoch 1 and (
A.27
), and the last inequality is because
\epsilon_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}
as long as
T
is large. It follows that with probability at least
1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}
,
i
would not be selected when
\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}
for all
q>1
. Since
\operatorname{Leb}(D^{\prime})>c^{\prime\prime}>0
, we have there exists some constant
\kappa>0
such that
\mathbb{P}(\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime})>\kappa
. Recall that
\mathcal{U}_{t}
denotes the event of unfairness at time
t
, i.e., (
4.3
) holds, then
\mathcal{U}_{t}
happens when
\pi_{t}\neq i
and
\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}
. Thus, for all
t>|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\kappa\left(1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}\right)\geq\frac{\kappa}{2},

which renders Algorithm
2
persistently unfair.

The above discussion gives that under event
\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
i
would not be selected for all
t>T/2>|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|
and all
\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}
. Then it holds that under event
\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
\{\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}_{i}\}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{t}
. Since
\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}_{i})
is i.i.d. Bernoulli random variable and independent of
\mathcal{G}_{1}
when
t>T/2>|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\frac{\kappa}{4}T\mid\mathcal{G}_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}_{i})\geq\frac{\kappa}{4}T\mid\mathcal{G}_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}_{i})\geq\frac{\kappa}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}_{i})-\mathbb{E}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in D^{\prime}_{i}))\geq-\frac{\kappa}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{4}T}.

As discussed above,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{G}_{1})\geq 1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}
, then by taking union bound we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\frac{\kappa}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\frac{\kappa}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{4}T}-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}\geq 1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)+1}{T}.

Now we can check the regret after corruption. Let the corrupted estimators be
\widehat{f}^{C}_{q,k}(\bm{x})
for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
. For reference, we denote the ideal estimators trained from the exact non-corrupted data (although not accessible) as
\widehat{f}_{q,k}(\bm{x})
. Note that although the attacker only corrupt data points collected from
D\subset\mathcal{R}_{i}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j}
for arm
i
, the inaccurate estimation for arm
i
in turn also influences the data collection process for all other arms. However, based on the properties of local polynomial estimators, the bandwidth
H_{q,k}
determines the sample radius for the point being estimated, so samples outside this region have no influence on the estimate at that point. We choose a fixed region
E
that strictly covers
D
such that
D\subset E\subset\mathcal{R}_{i}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j}
with
\mathbb{P}\left(X\in(\mathcal{R}_{i}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j})\setminus E\right)>\kappa^{\prime}
. Thus, if
\max_{k\in\{i,j\}}H_{q,k}\rightarrow 0
, the corrupted estimators satisfy, for all
k\in\mathcal{K}=\{i,j\}
and
q\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\widehat{f}^{C}_{q,k}(\bm{x})=\widehat{f}_{q,k}(\bm{x})\text{ for all }\bm{x}\notin E,

which implies, if
\mathcal{K}^{C}_{q-1,u(\bm{x})}=\mathcal{K}_{q-1,u(\bm{x})}\text{ for all }\bm{x}\notin E
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{C}_{q,u(\bm{x})}=\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}\text{ for all }\bm{x}\notin E,

and since the algorithm choose
\pi_{t}\in\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x}_{t})}
with equal probability, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{t}^{C}|\bm{x}_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\pi_{t}|\bm{x}_{t}\text{ when }\bm{x}_{t}\notin E\text{ and }t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{q}.

Assume
\max_{q\geq 1}\max_{k\in\{i,j\}}H_{q,k}\rightarrow 0
. Then by induction, since
\mathcal{K}^{C}_{0,u(\bm{x})}=\mathcal{K}_{0,u(\bm{x})}=\{i,j\}
, it follows that for all
t
,
\pi_{t}^{C}|\bm{x}_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\pi_{t}|\bm{x}_{t}\text{ when }\bm{x}_{t}\notin E.
Denote the regret after corruption as
R_{T}^{C}
and uncorrupted regret as
R_{T}
, we have that

\displaystyle R_{T}^{C}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}^{C}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}^{C}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in E)\biggr]+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}^{C}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\notin E)\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}^{C}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\notin E)\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\notin E)\biggr]\leq R_{T},

where the third equality is because when
\bm{x}_{t}\in E
,
\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}^{C}}(\bm{x}_{t})=0
no matter of
\pi_{t}^{C}
. It remains to bound the probability of
\max_{q\geq 1}\max_{k\in\{i,j\}}H_{q,k}\rightarrow 0
, which is equivalent to
\min_{q\geq 1}\min_{k\in\{i,j\}}N_{q,k}\rightarrow\infty
. This can be regarded as measuring the sample size collected from
\bm{x}_{t}\notin E
when no corruption exists. Following the same proof of Proposition
3.7
, as long as
\mathbb{P}\left(X\in(\mathcal{R}_{i}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j})\setminus E\right)>\kappa^{\prime}
, it can be concluded that
\mathbb{P}(\lim_{T\to\infty}\max_{q\geq 1}\max_{k\in\{i,j\}}H_{q,k}=0)\geq 1-\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{T})
. Thus, by law of total expectation,

\displaystyle R_{T}^{C}\leq R_{T}+\widetilde{O}(1).

This finishes the proof.
\square

A.3.2
Proof of Theorem
4.2

For a persistently unfair algorithm, the expected cumulative regret can be written as

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\biggr]\geq\sum_{t=N}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\biggr]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t=N}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mid\mathcal{U}_{t}\biggr]\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{U}_{t})

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}\sum_{t=N}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mid\mathcal{U}_{t}\biggr]

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}\sum_{t=N}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mid\mathcal{U}_{t},\pi_{t}=j\biggr]\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=j|\mathcal{U}_{t})

\displaystyle\geq c_{1}c_{2}\sum_{t=N}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mid\mathcal{U}_{t},\pi_{t}=j\biggr].

(A.28)

Suppose the adversary executes an attack by choosing an arm
i\in\mathcal{K}
and setting
c_{t}=-\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}2\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}-1
if the played arm is
\pi_{t}=i
, for all
t\leq t_{0}
. As a result, the algorithm only observes a masked reward function from arm
i
during this period. Specifically, the player observes
y_{i,t}=f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})-\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}2\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}-1+\varepsilon_{i,t}=\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x}_{t})+\varepsilon_{i,t}
for all
t\leq t_{0}
, where
\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}
represents the masked reward function. Thus, for any
k\neq i
,

\displaystyle f_{k}^{*}(\bm{x}_{t})-\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x}_{t})

\displaystyle\geq-\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}-f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})+\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}2\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}+1

\displaystyle\geq-\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}-\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}+\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}2\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}+1

\displaystyle=1.

(A.29)

(i) Consider Algorithm
1
. Let
|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}|=t_{0}=C_{a}\log T
. As a result, the budget is upper bounded by
C\leq|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}|(\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}2\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}+1)\lesssim\log T=\widetilde{O}(1)
. Then we need to prove that, with high probability, arm
i
would not be selected any more for
t>|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}|
. Note that the initial estimation
\widehat{\beta}_{k,0}
is trained only based on observations from the corrupted function
\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}
, thus in the following, we use
\widetilde{\beta}_{i}
to replace
{\beta}_{i}
. Without loss of generality, we let
h<1
(since if Assumption
3
holds for a larger
h
, it will automatically hold for smaller
h
). In the first epoch, because of the nature of random exploration, Proposition
A.1
that controls the tail probability of event
\mathcal{B}
is still valid. Under event
\mathcal{B}
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|(\widehat{\beta_{i}}(\mathcal{I}_{i,0})-\widetilde{\beta_{i}})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|>-\frac{h}{4}

and for any
l\in\mathcal{K}\setminus\{i\}
,

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|(\widehat{\beta_{l}}(\mathcal{I}_{l,0})-\beta_{l})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|>-\frac{h}{4},

where the first inequality is because
y_{i,t}=\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x}_{t})+\varepsilon_{i,t}=\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\beta}_{i}+\varepsilon_{i,t}
for
t\in\mathcal{I}_{i,0}
.
Thus, for any
l\in\mathcal{K}\setminus\{i\}
and
t\geq t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}_{l,0}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}_{i,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}_{l,0}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{l}+\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{l}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\beta}_{i}+\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\beta}_{i}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}_{i,0}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{h}{4}+1-\frac{h}{4}>\frac{h}{2},

which rules out the chance that
i
is chosen into
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{\bm{x}_{t}}
. By Assumption
3
,
\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{i}
implies
f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\geq f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x}_{t})+h
, and
\mathbb{P}(\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{i})\geq\widetilde{p}
. Moreover, by the selection rule, together with Proposition
A.1
, for all
t>|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}|
,
\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=i\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}^{+})=0
happens with probability at least
1-4KT^{-4}
. It can be further concluded that there exists an arm
j\in\widehat{\mathcal{K}}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=i\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}^{+})=0<\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=j\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}^{+})-\frac{1}{K}
happens with probability at least
1-4KT^{-4}
. Recall that
\mathcal{U}_{t}
denotes the event of unfairness at time
t
, i.e., (
4.3
) holds. Combining the above, we have for all
t>|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}|
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{U}_{t}\cap\{f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\geq f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x}_{t})+h\})\geq\widetilde{p}+(1-4KT^{-4})-1\geq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2},

which renders Algorithm
1
persistently unfair, and by (
A.3.2
), we have

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\sum_{t=N}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mid\mathcal{U}_{t},\pi_{t}=j\biggr]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t=N}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\left(f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mid\mathcal{U}_{t},\pi_{t}=j\biggr]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t=N}^{T}\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2}h\gtrsim T.

The above discussion gives that under event
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=i\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}^{+})=0
for all
t>T/2>|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}|
. Then it holds that under event
\mathcal{B}
,
\{\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{i}\}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{t}
. Since
\mathbb{I}(\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{i})
is i.i.d. Bernoulli random variable and independent of
\mathcal{B}
when
t>T/2>|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}|
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}T\mid\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{i})\geq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}T\mid\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{i})\geq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{i})-\mathbb{E}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{i}))\geq-\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{4}T}.

As discussed,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B})\geq 1-4KT^{-4}
, then by taking union bound we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}T)\geq\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}T)\geq 1-e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{4}T}-4KT^{-4}\geq 1-\frac{4K+1}{T^{4}}.

(ii) Consider Algorithm
2
. Let
t_{0}=|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|=\left\lceil\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{4\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}+\frac{K^{2}}{2p^{*2}}\log T\right\rceil
. Thus, the budget is
C\leq|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|(\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}2\|f_{k}^{*}\|_{\infty}+1)\lesssim(\log T)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}=\widetilde{O}(1)
.

The estimators trained by samples collected from epoch 1 exhibit inherently good properties guaranteed by Assumption
4
. Following the same steps in bounding the tail probability of event
\mathcal{G}_{1}
in Proposition
3.7
, we have with probability at least
1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}
, for any
l\in\mathcal{K}\setminus\{i\}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{1,l}(\bm{x}_{t})-\widehat{f}_{1,i}(\bm{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=(\widehat{f}_{1,l}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{l}(\bm{x}_{t}))+f^{*}_{l}(\bm{x}_{t})-\widetilde{f}^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})-(\widehat{f}_{1,i}(\bm{x}_{t})-\widetilde{f}^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t}))

\displaystyle\geq-1/2\epsilon_{1}+1-1/2\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\geq 2\epsilon_{1},

where the second inequality is because samples are collected from the masked function
\widetilde{f}_{i}^{*}(\bm{x})
during epoch 1, and the last inequality is because
\epsilon_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}
as long as
T
is large. It follows that with probability at least
1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}
,
i\notin\mathcal{K}_{1,j}
for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{C}|\}
and thus
i
would not be selected for all
q>1
.

By the assumption that there exists an arm
k\in\mathcal{K}
satisfying
\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\left(f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})-\max_{j\neq k}f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x})\right)>c^{\prime\prime}
for some positive constant
c^{\prime\prime}
, let the adversary choose
i=k
. Define
Q^{\prime}_{i}=\{\bm{x}:f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x})-\max_{j\neq i}f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x}))>\frac{1}{2}c^{\prime\prime}\}
. Since
f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x})-\max_{j\neq i}f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x})
is a continuous function, and the probability density of
\bm{x}
is bounded away from zero by Assumption
4
, there exists some constant
\kappa>0
such that
\mathbb{P}(\bm{x}\in Q^{\prime}_{i})>\kappa
.

Obviously,
\mathcal{U}_{t}
happens when
\pi_{t}\neq i
and
\bm{x}_{t}\in Q^{\prime}_{i}
. Thus, for all
t>|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{U}_{t}\cap\{f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x})-\max_{j\neq i}f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x}))>\frac{1}{2}c^{\prime\prime}\})\geq\kappa+1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}-1\geq\frac{\kappa^{*}}{2},

which renders Algorithm
2
persistently unfair, and by (
A.3.2
), we have

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\sum_{t=N}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mid\mathcal{U}_{t},\pi_{t}=j\biggr]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t=N}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\left(f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mid\mathcal{U}_{t},\pi_{t}=j\biggr]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t=N}^{T}\frac{\kappa^{*}}{2}\frac{c^{\prime\prime}}{2}\gtrsim T.

The above discussion gives that under event
\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
i
would not be selected for all
t>T/2>|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|
and all
\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}
. Then it holds that under event
\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
\{\bm{x}_{t}\in Q^{\prime}_{i}\}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{t}
. Since
\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in Q^{\prime}_{i})
is i.i.d. Bernoulli random variable and independent of
\mathcal{G}_{1}
when
t>T/2>|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}|
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\frac{\kappa^{*}}{4}T\mid\mathcal{G}_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in Q^{\prime}_{i})\geq\frac{\kappa^{*}}{4}T\mid\mathcal{G}_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in Q^{\prime}_{i})\geq\frac{\kappa^{*}}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in Q^{\prime}_{i})-\mathbb{E}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\bm{x}_{t}\in Q^{\prime}_{i}))\geq-\frac{\kappa^{*}}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-\frac{\kappa^{*2}}{4}T}.

As discussed above,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{G}_{1})\geq 1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}
, then by taking union bound we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\frac{\kappa^{*}}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\sum_{t=T/2}^{T}\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{U}_{t})\geq\frac{\kappa^{*}}{4}T)

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-\frac{\kappa^{*2}}{4}T}-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)}{T}\geq 1-\frac{K\left(5+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)+1}{T}.

\square

A.4
Proofs in Section
5

A.4.1
Supporting Lemmas and Theorems

We first list several lemmas that will be used in this section. Note that for all
1\leq q\leq Q^{RS}
, we set

\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}=(2^{-q}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\vee T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}.

In addition,
\lambda_{0}
and
M_{\beta}
are as in Assumption
8
.

Lemma A.10
.

When
T>e^{C_{K}}\vee e^{4(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})^{2}}
,
Q^{RS}\leq\lceil\frac{\beta}{(2\beta+d)\log 2}\log(\frac{Tp^{*}}{2K}\left(\log T\right)^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}+\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}})\rceil\wedge\lceil\frac{Tp^{*}}{2K}\left(\log T\right)^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}+\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}C^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}\rceil
, and for all
1\leq q\leq Q^{RS}
,
\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}\geq(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})\delta_{A}.

Lemma A.11
.

When
\max_{i,j\in\mathcal{K}}\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\Delta_{i,j}(\bm{x})\leq T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}}CT^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}
, Under event
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q}^{RS}
,
S_{q,k}^{RS}=\mathcal{X}
for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
.

Lemma A.12
.

For any
1\leq q\leq Q-1
, and integers
n_{q,k}
that satisfy
n_{q,k}\geq\left(\frac{12(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})\sqrt{M_{\beta}}Lv_{d}p_{\max}}{p^{*}\lambda_{0}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta}}
, under Assumptions
4
-
6
, assume that
S_{q,k}^{RS}
is weakly
(\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}},H_{q,k}^{RS})
-regular at all
\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\cap G
, then for sufficiently large
T
, the estimator
\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}
based on samples in the
q
-th epoch satisfies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\sup_{\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}}\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}}n_{q,k}^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}C\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K\delta_{A}^{-d}\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime 2}\right).

A.4.2
Proof of Proposition
5.1

First, we decompose
(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}_{t}
by

\displaystyle\left|(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}_{t}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{Y}-\beta_{k}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}_{t}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}(Z\beta_{k}+\bm{c}+\bm{\varepsilon})-\beta_{k}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}(Z\beta_{k}+\bm{\varepsilon})-\beta_{k}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}_{t}\right|+\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}_{t}\right|

(A.30)

where
\bm{c}=[c_{j}]_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{k,0}}^{\mathrm{T}}
, and
\bm{\varepsilon}=[\bm{\varepsilon}]_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{k,0}}^{\mathrm{T}}
.

Apparently, the first term is
\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}(Z\beta_{k}+\bm{\varepsilon})-\beta_{k}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}_{t}\right|=\left|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}_{t}\right|
. Applying the same steps in the proof of Proposition
A.1
except for letting
\chi=\frac{h}{8}
, with the assumptions on
C_{a}
, it can be obtained that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\left|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|\geq\frac{h}{8})\leq 4KT^{-4}.

(A.31)

Then we focus on the second term in (
A.4.2
). By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it holds that

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|(Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\|_{2}\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\bm{z}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\|_{2}\sqrt{dr^{2}}

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|^{-1}\|\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\|_{2}\sqrt{dr^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0}))\|\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\bm{z}_{j}c_{j}\|_{2}\sqrt{dr^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0}))\sqrt{dr^{2}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}|c_{j}|\|\bm{z}_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})){dr^{2}}C,

(A.32)

where the last inequality is because
\|\bm{z}_{j}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{dr^{2}}
and
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}|c_{j}|\leq C
. Define the event
\mathcal{A}=\{|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|\geq\frac{1}{2K}|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|\}
. By (
A.5.1
), under event
\mathcal{A}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0}))\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}{4K}\right]\leq 2T^{-4},

(A.33)

and (
A.61
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{A}\right]\geq 1-\frac{1}{T^{4}}.

Therefore, conditioned on event
\mathcal{A}
, (
A.4.2
) and (
A.33
) gives that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|\leq\frac{2K}{|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{RL}|}\frac{4K}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}dr^{2}C\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})){dr^{2}}C\leq\frac{2K}{|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{RL}|}\frac{4K}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}dr^{2}C\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-2T^{-4}.

Consequently, without conditioning on
\mathcal{A}
, we obtain the unconditional probability bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|\leq\frac{2K}{|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{RL}|}\frac{4K}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}dr^{2}C\right)\geq 1-3T^{-4},

(A.34)

which further implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|\geq\frac{h}{8}\right)\leq 3T^{-4},

since
|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{RL}|>C_{a}\log T+\frac{64K^{2}dr^{2}}{h\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}C
gives
\frac{2K}{|{\mathcal{T}}_{0}^{RL}|}\frac{4K}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}dr^{2}C\leq\frac{h}{8}
.

Taking union bounds over
k\in\mathcal{K}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|\geq\frac{h}{8}\right)\leq 3KT^{-4}.

(A.35)

Combining (
A.4.2
), (
A.31
) and (
A.35
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\left|(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|\geq\frac{h}{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\left|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|\geq\frac{h}{8}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|\geq\frac{h}{8}\right)\leq 7KT^{-4},

which finishes the proof.
\square

A.4.3
Proof of Proposition
5.2

Similar to (
A.4.2
) and (
A.4.2
), we can decompose
\left|(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|
by

\displaystyle\left|(\widetilde{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{Y}-\beta_{k}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}+\left((Z^{\mathrm{T}}Z)^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{c}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}\right|+|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})){dr^{2}}C.

When
|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|<t\leq 2|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|
, since
\mathcal{I}_{k,0}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{k,t}
, it is obvious that
|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|\leq|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|
and
|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0}))\leq|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t}))
, which leads to

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})){dr^{2}}C\leq|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})){dr^{2}}C.

The RHS of the above inequality has been bounded by (
A.34
), then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})){dr^{2}}C<\frac{h}{8})\geq\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})){dr^{2}}C\leq\frac{h}{8})\geq 1-3T^{-4}

Since
t\leq 2|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|
implies
\frac{\epsilon_{t+1}}{4}=\frac{C_{b}}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{t+1}}+\frac{1}{4}(\frac{192K^{2}dr^{2}}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}\vee\frac{32dr^{2}}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}^{2}})\frac{C}{t+1}\geq\frac{h}{8}
, we further have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})){dr^{2}}C\leq\frac{C_{b}}{4}\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{t+1}}+\frac{1}{4}(\frac{192K^{2}dr^{2}}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}\vee\frac{32dr^{2}}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}^{2}})\frac{C}{t+1})\geq 1-3T^{-4}.

Therefore, when
|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|<t\leq 2|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{\epsilon^{RL}_{t+1}}{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{\epsilon^{RL}_{t+1}}{4})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})){dr^{2}}C\geq\frac{\epsilon^{RL}_{t+1}}{4})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 7KT^{-4},

where the last inequality is given by (
A.31
).

When
t>2|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|
, i.e.,
t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|\geq\frac{t+1}{2}
, under event
\mathcal{B}
, by (
A.63
) and (
A.64
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}(t+1)\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|)\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|)}\leq e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{2}(C_{a}\log T+\frac{64K^{2}dr^{2}}{h\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}C)}\leq T^{-4},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t}))\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}{8}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{8}\widetilde{C}\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{*}}\right)t+\log d\right]+\exp\left[-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{4}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2T^{-4}.

Thus, under event
\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})){dr^{2}}C\geq\frac{16dr^{2}}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}^{2}}\frac{C}{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}(t+1)\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t}))\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}{8}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3T^{-4}.

Taking union bound with respect to all arms
k\in{\mathcal{K}}
and combining with the fact that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B})\geq 1-7KT^{-4}
given in Proposition
5.1
, it further yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})){dr^{2}}C\geq\frac{16dr^{2}}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}^{2}}\frac{C}{t+1})\leq 10KT^{-4}.

By Proposition
A.2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{C_{b}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{t}})\leq 8KT^{-4}.

Putting the results together, we can conclude that when
t>2|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{\epsilon^{RL}_{t+1}}{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{C_{b}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{t+1}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|^{-1}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})){dr^{2}}C\geq\frac{32dr^{2}}{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}^{2}}\frac{C}{2(t+1)})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 15KT^{-4}.

In conclusion, for all
t>|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|
,
\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{\epsilon^{RL}_{t+1}}{2})\leq 15KT^{-4}
.
\square

A.4.4
Proof of Theorem
5.3

The reasoning parallels that of proof of Theorem
3.1
. Thus, it suffices to prove that

\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}|(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|<\frac{h}{4})\geq 1-15KT^{-4}

and

\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}|(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t-1})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|<\frac{\epsilon^{RL}_{t}}{2})\geq 1-15KT^{-4},

which are guaranteed by Proposition
5.1
and Proposition
5.2
.
\square

A.4.5
Proof of Theorem
5.4

Denote the event
\mathcal{W}_{t}^{RL}=\{\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}|(\widehat{\beta}^{RL}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t-1})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}_{t}|<\frac{\epsilon^{RL}_{q}}{2}\}
. As in the proof of Theorem
3.2
, we decompose the cumulative regret into three parts:

\displaystyle R_{T}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}}\right)+\sum_{t=|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|+1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left((\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}})\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{W}_{t}^{RL})\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{t=|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|+1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left((\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}})\mathbb{I}((\mathcal{W}_{t}^{RL})^{C})\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle R_{1}+R_{2}+R_{3}.

(A.36)

First, since
\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}}=O(1)
because all terms are bounded, it is obvious that

\displaystyle R_{1}\lesssim|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|\lesssim\log T+C.

(A.37)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle R_{3}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|+1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left((\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}})\mathbb{I}((\mathcal{W}_{t}^{RL})^{C})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{t=|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|+1}^{T}\mathbb{P}(\left(\mathcal{W}_{t}^{RL})^{C}\right)\leq T(15KT^{-4})=O(1).

(A.38)

It remains to bound
R_{2}
. If
\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{k,0}\geq\max\limits_{l\in\mathcal{K}\setminus\{k\}}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}^{RL}_{l,0}+h/2
, we have proved that
\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=\arg\max_{l\in\mathcal{K}}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{l})\geq 1-7KT^{-4}
, which leads to
\mathbb{E}\left((\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}})\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{W}_{t}^{RL})\right)\lesssim 7KT^{-4}
. Otherwise, following the same reasoning in the proof of Theorem
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left((\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}})\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{W}_{t}^{RL})\right)\leq

\displaystyle K\epsilon^{RL}_{t}\mathbb{P}(0<(\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}})\leq K\epsilon^{RL}_{t})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle K^{4}(\epsilon^{RL}_{t})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\log T}{t}+\frac{C^{2}}{t^{2}},

where the second inequality is by taking union bound over all pair of arms on the margin condition, which implies
\mathbb{P}(0<(\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}})\leq K\epsilon^{RL}_{t})\lesssim K^{3}\epsilon^{RL}_{t}
, and the last inequality is by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
In conclusion, under both cases,

\mathbb{E}\left((\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}})\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{W}_{t}^{RL})\right)\lesssim\frac{\log T}{t}+\frac{C^{2}}{t^{2}},

which implies

\displaystyle R_{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|+1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left((\max_{k}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{\pi_{t}})\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{W}_{t}^{RL})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{t=|\mathcal{T}_{0}^{RL}|+1}^{T}\left(\frac{\log T}{t}+\frac{C^{2}}{t^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\log T}{t}+\sum_{t=\log T+C}^{T}\frac{C^{2}}{t^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\log^{2}T+\frac{C^{2}}{\log T+C}\lesssim\log^{2}T+C.

(A.39)

By plugging the bounds in (
A.37
), (
A.4.5
) and (
A.4.5
) to (
A.4.5
), we obtain
R_{T}=O(\log^{2}T+C)
, which finishes the proof.
\square

A.4.6
Proof of Theorem
5.5

The lower bound on the cumulative regret incurred by any admissible policy for the problem class defined by Assumptions
1
through
3
under corrupted observation model with budget
C
is established as follows.

For simplicity, we ignore the intercept term and noise term (treated as noiseless), and let the expected reward model be
f_{k}(x)=\beta_{k}x
with
x\in[-1,1]
uniformly i.i.d distributed. Consider an instance with two arms denoted as 1 and 2. Apparently, Assumptions
1
to
3
are satisfied. Let
\beta_{1}=0
and
\beta_{2}=a
, where
a
is a random variable satisfying
\mathbb{P}(a=1)=\frac{1}{2}
and
\mathbb{P}(a=-1)=\frac{1}{2}
. Suppose that the adversary shifts every observed reward to zero until its budget burns out, which would last
T_{C}=\Omega(C)
rounds. Therefore, any admissible policy would make decisions independent on the value of
a
, which implies that
a
and
\pi_{t}
is independent when
t<T_{c}
. Consider the problem set as
\mathcal{P}=\{(\beta_{1}=0,\beta_{2}=1),(\beta_{1}=0,\beta_{2}=-1)\}
. Hence, for any admissible policy, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k=1,2}\beta_{k}x_{t}-\beta_{\pi_{t}}x_{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{a}\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k=1,2}\beta_{k}x_{t}-\beta_{\pi_{t}}x_{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T_{C}}\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\max_{k=1,2}\beta_{k}x_{t}-\beta_{\pi_{t}}x_{t}\right)\bigg|a=1,\pi_{t}=1,x_{t}>\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathbb{P}(a=1,\pi_{t}=1,x_{t}>1/2)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T_{C}}\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\max_{k=1,2}\beta_{k}x_{t}-\beta_{\pi_{t}}x_{t}\right)\bigg|a=-1,\pi_{t}=2,x_{t}>\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathbb{P}(a=-1,\pi_{t}=2,x_{t}>1/2).

(A.40)

For
t\leq T_{C}
, if
a=1,\pi_{t}=1,x_{t}>\frac{1}{2}
, the optimal arm should be 2, while the player chooses arm 1, with the regret
\beta_{2}\bm{x}_{t}-0=a\bm{x}_{t}>1/2
. Similarly, for
t\leq T_{C}
, if
a=-1,\pi_{t}=2,x_{t}>1/2
, the optimal arm should be 1, while the player chooses arm 2, with
0-\beta_{2}\bm{x}_{t}=-a\bm{x}_{t}>1/2
. Therefore, (
A.4.6
) implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k=1,2}\beta_{k}x_{t}-\beta_{\pi_{t}}x_{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{C}}\left(\mathbb{P}(a=1,\pi_{t}=1,x_{t}>1/2)+\mathbb{P}(a=-1,\pi_{t}=2,x_{t}>1/2)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{C}}\left(\mathbb{P}(a=1)\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=1,x_{t}>1/2)+\mathbb{P}(a=-1)\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=2,x_{t}>1/2)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{C}}\mathbb{P}(x_{t}>1/2)=\frac{1}{16}T_{C}=\Omega(C),

(A.41)

where the first equality is by the independence of
a
and
\pi_{t}
. It can be seen from (
A.4.6
) that
R_{T}=\Omega(C)
. Moreover, by Theorem
3.3
, we have that any admissible policy will incur a cumulative regret of at least
R_{T}=\Omega(\log^{2}T)
. Combining this with the preceding analysis, the lower bound is established as
R_{T}=\Omega(\log^{2}T)\vee\Omega(C)=\Omega(\log^{2}T+C)
. This finishes the proof.
\square

A.4.7
Proof of Proposition
5.6

Since Algorithm
4
differs from Algorithm
2
only in its setting of epoch length and error threshold, in this proof, we only state the necessarily changed steps in the proof of Proposition
3.6
. Specifically, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle K\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS}\leq

\displaystyle c^{\prime\prime},

(A.42)

\displaystyle\frac{(\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS})^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}}{H_{q,k}^{RS}}\leq

\displaystyle 1-(1-\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}})^{\frac{1}{d}},

(A.43)

for
q>1
. Note that when
q=1
, Algorithm
4
defaults to uniform exploration throughout the entire horizon, which implies that the statement in Proposition
5.6
automatically holds, as all arms receive equal sampling probability.

Proof of (
A.42
).
Note that

C|{\mathcal{T}}_{q-1}^{RS}|^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\leq C\left(\frac{C_{K}}{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}\right)=o(1),

which implies that

\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS}=(2^{-q+1}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\vee T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}+C|{\mathcal{T}}_{q-1}^{RS}|^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}=o(1).

Hence, for sufficiently large
T
, (
A.42
) holds.

Proof of (
A.43
).

Apparently,
f_{i}^{*}(\bm{x}_{1})-f_{k}^{*}(\bm{x}_{1})\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{RS}\lesssim C^{-\frac{1}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}
.
Since
H_{q,k}^{RS}=(N_{q,k}^{RS})^{-1/(2\beta+d)}\geq|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-1/(2\beta+d)}
and according to the definition of
\epsilon_{q}^{RS}
and
H_{q,k}^{RS}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS})^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}}{H_{q,k}^{RS}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(2^{-q\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}}{\left(\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}+\frac{K^{2}}{2p^{*2}}\log T\right)^{-1/(2\beta+d)}}

\displaystyle+\frac{T^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta+d}}}{|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-1/(2\beta+d)}}+\frac{C^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta+d}}}{|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-1/(2\beta+d)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log T)^{-\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta}}(2^{q}\vee C^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})^{(\frac{1-\beta^{\prime}}{\beta})}+T^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1}{2\beta+d}}+(\log T)^{-\frac{2\beta^{\prime}-1}{\beta^{\prime}-1}},

where the third term in the second inequality is because
|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|\gtrsim(\log T)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}C^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}
, then
\frac{C^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta+d}}}{|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-1/(2\beta+d)}}=C^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-\frac{2\beta^{\prime}-1}{2\beta+d}}\lesssim(\log T)^{-\frac{2\beta^{\prime}-1}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}
.
This implies that (
A.43
) holds when
T
is large enough.

By leveraging (
A.42
) and (
A.43
), and taking the similar steps as in the proof of Proposition
3.6
, it follows that for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
,
S_{q,k}^{RS}
is weakly
(\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}},H_{q,k}^{RS})
-regular at all
\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\cap G
.
\square

A.4.8
Proof of Proposition
5.7

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{RS}_{q}=\left\{\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}}N_{q,k}^{RS}\geq\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log(T\delta_{A}^{-d})}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{RS}_{q}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\text{(i) }\text{ for all }k\in\mathcal{K},S_{q,k}^{RS}\text{ is weakly }(\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}},H_{q,k}^{RS})\text{-regular at all }\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\cap G\\
&\text{(ii) }\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|\leq\epsilon_{q}^{RS}/2\text{ for all }\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\text{ and }k\in\mathcal{K}\end{aligned}\right\}.

We first show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((\mathcal{G}_{q}^{RS})^{C}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{RS}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{RS}_{q}\right)\leq\frac{\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)K}{T},

(A.44)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((\mathcal{M}^{RS}_{q})^{C}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{RS}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{RS}_{q-1}\right)\leq\frac{K}{T}.

(A.45)

Proof of (
A.44
).

Recall that we let
\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}=(2^{-q}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\vee T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}
. When
T
is sufficiently large, by direct computation, we have that under event
\mathcal{M}_{q}^{RS}
,

\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}}N_{q,k}^{RS}\geq\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log(T\delta_{A}^{-d})}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\geq\left(\frac{12(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})\sqrt{M_{\beta}}Lv_{d}p_{\max}}{p^{*}\lambda_{0}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta}}.

By Proposition
5.6
, under event
\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{RS}_{q-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{RS}_{q}
, for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
,
S_{q,k}^{RS}
is weakly
(\frac{c_{0}}{2^{d}},H_{q,k}^{RS})
-regular at all
\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\cap G
, which proves (i) of event
\mathcal{G}_{q}^{RS}
. Then the conditions in Lemma
A.12
are satisfied.

Since

\displaystyle\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log(T\delta_{A}^{-d})}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\geq\frac{p^{*}}{4K}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|,

the tail probability in Lemma
A.12
now can be bounded as

\displaystyle K\delta_{A}^{-d}\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime 2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K\delta_{A}^{-d}\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)\exp\left(-(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log(T\delta_{A}^{-d})\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}(4^{-q}\wedge C^{-\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(4+2M_{\beta}^{2}\right)K}{T},

since
n_{q,k}
is lower bounded by
\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log(T\delta_{A}^{-d})}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}
.

Noting that the error term

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}}n_{q,k}^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}C

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left((2^{-q}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\vee T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\right)+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}}n_{q,k}^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}C

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left((2^{-q}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\vee T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}+\frac{2\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}}C(\frac{p^{*}}{4K}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\right)=\epsilon_{q}^{RS}/2,

we now complete the proof.

Proof of (
A.45
).
Note that the proof of Lemma
A.5
is also valid for the adversarial corruption case, and thus
\mathcal{R}_{k}\subseteq S_{q,k}^{RS}
for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
. Together with Assumptions
4
and
6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(N_{q,k}^{RS}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}}\mathbb{I}\left\{\pi_{t}=k\right\}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{RS}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{RS}_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}}\mathbb{P}\left(\pi_{t}=k\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{RS}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{RS}_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}}\mathbb{P}\left(\pi_{t}=k,\bm{x}_{t}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{RS}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{RS}_{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{K}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}}\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{R}_{k}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{RS}_{q-1},\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{RS}_{q-1}\right)\geq\frac{p^{*}}{K}|\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}|.

When
q>1
, the decisions in the epoch
q
are only dependent on the history samples, thus it is obvious that
{\mathcal{T}}_{q,k}^{RS}
are i.i.d. conditional on
\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}^{RS}\}_{h=1}^{q-1}
. Together with Hoeffding’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(N_{q,k}^{RS}<\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},\left\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}^{RS}\right\}_{h=1}^{q-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\left(N_{q,k}^{RS}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS}\right)-N_{q,k}^{RS}>\frac{p^{*}}{K}|\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}|

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},\left\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}^{RS}\right\}_{h=1}^{q-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{2}{|\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}|}\left[\frac{p^{*}}{K}|\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}|-\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{2}{|\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}|}\left(\frac{p^{*2}}{K^{2}}|\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}|^{2}-\frac{2p^{*}}{K}|\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}|\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{2p^{*2}}{K^{2}}|\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}|+\frac{4p^{*}}{K}\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\right).

(A.46)

Since

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}+\frac{K^{2}}{2p^{*2}}\log T\right\rceil

\displaystyle>\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}+\frac{K^{2}}{2p^{*2}}\log T,

we have

\displaystyle\frac{2p^{*2}}{K^{2}}|\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}|-\frac{4p^{*}}{K}\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\geq\log T,

which, together with (
A.4.8
), implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(N_{q,k}^{RS}<\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\mid\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},\left\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}^{RS}\right\}_{h=1}^{q-1}\right)\leq\frac{1}{T}.

In that way, when marginalizing over
\{\bigcup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{T}}_{h,k}^{RS}\}_{h=1}^{q-1}
and taking the union bound, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}}N_{q,k}^{RS}<\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{(2\beta+d)\frac{2\beta-\beta^{\prime}+1}{2\beta(\beta^{\prime}-1)}}\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS}\right)\leq\frac{K}{T}.

Finishing the proof of Proposition
5.7
.
This can be done by repeating the proof of (
3.4
) in Proposition
3.7
, thus we omit it for simplicity.
\square

A.4.9
Proof of Theorem
5.8

When
|{\mathcal{T}}_{Q^{RS}-1}^{RS}|\leq T
, i.e.,
Q^{RS}>1
, the reasoning parallels that of proof of Theorem
3.8
, which is not affected by the exact value of error threshold. Otherwise,
Q^{RS}=1
also implies that Algorithm
4
is fair for sure.
\square

A.4.10
Proof of Theorem
5.9

When
Q^{RS}=1
, i.e.,
|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}^{RS}|\geq T
, we have
R_{T}=O(T)
. When
Q^{RS}>1
,
|{\mathcal{T}}_{1}^{RS}|<T
implies
C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\lesssim T
. Then similar to the proof of Theorem
3.9
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q=1}^{Q^{RS}}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|(\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS})^{\alpha+1}+\sum_{q=1}^{Q^{RS}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q}^{RS}}\frac{(q-1)}{T}

\displaystyle:=J_{1}+J_{2}.

Since
Q^{RS}\lesssim\log T
and
\sum_{q}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|=T
, we have

\displaystyle J_{2}\lesssim\log T.

For the first sum
J_{1}
, note that
\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS}\lesssim|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}+C|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}
(up to
\log T
factors). Then
(\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS})^{\alpha+1}\lesssim|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}(\alpha+1)}+C^{\alpha+1}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}(\alpha+1)}
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{1-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}(\alpha+1)}+C^{\alpha+1}\sum_{q}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{1-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}(\alpha+1)}

\displaystyle=J_{1,1}+J_{1,2}.

Case 1:
\alpha\beta\leq d/2
. Ignoring logs for simplicity gives a geometric series
|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|\asymp(\gamma^{q})^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}
. Then direct computation shows
J_{1,1}\lesssim T^{1-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}(\alpha+1)}
and
J_{1,2}\lesssim C^{1+\alpha}T^{1-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}(\alpha+1)}
.

Case 2:
\alpha\beta>d/2
. Then direct computation shows
J_{1,1}\lesssim T^{1-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}(\alpha+1)}
and

\displaystyle J_{1,2}\lesssim C^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}+\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}=C^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}+[\frac{d}{2\beta}-\alpha]\frac{1}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}.

\square

A.4.11
Proof of Theorem
5.10

The lower bound on the cumulative regret incurred by any admissible
(1-\delta)
-fair policy for the problem class defined by Assumptions
4
-
8
under corruption with budget
C
is established as follows. We modify the construction presented in the proof of Theorem 3 from
31
, which also aligns with the approach in
3
. Our proof technique and the specific instance we consider differ to better align with the robust smooth contextual bandit problem.
Let
\delta_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
be fixed. We set the instance parameters as

\displaystyle q=\left\lceil\left(4C_{\phi}\frac{T}{C}\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta+d}}\right\rceil,\quad m=\lceil q^{d-\alpha\beta}\rceil,\quad\omega=q^{-d},

where
T
is assumed to be sufficiently large.

Let
G_{q}=\left\{\left(\frac{2j_{1}+1}{2q},\dots,\frac{2j_{d}+1}{2q}\right):j_{i}\in\{0,\dots,q-1\},\,i=1,\dots,d\right\}
be the
d
-dimensional grid, with its
q^{d}
points enumerated as
