\{\bm{x}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{q^{d}}
. For any
\bm{x}\in[0,1]^{d}
, define
g_{q}(\bm{x})
as the unique point in
G_{q}
minimizing
\|\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}\|
(resolving ties by selecting the point closest to the origin). Let
\mathcal{X}_{i}=\mathrm{Cube}_{q}(\bm{x}_{i}):=\{\bm{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}:g_{q}(\bm{x}^{\prime})=\bm{x}_{i}\}
be hypercubes of edge length
1/q
centered at
\bm{x}_{i}
, and region
\mathcal{X}_{0}=[0,1]^{d}\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{X}_{i}
.

The density
\mu_{X}
of the covariate distribution
\mathbb{P}_{X}
is defined as

\mu_{X}(\bm{x})=\begin{cases}\frac{\omega}{\operatorname{Leb}(\mathcal{B}(0,\frac{1}{4q}))}&\text{if }\bm{x}\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{i},\frac{1}{4q})\\
\frac{1-m\omega}{\operatorname{Leb}(\mathcal{X}_{0})}&\text{if }\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{0}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus,
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}_{0}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{B}(\bm{x}_{i},\frac{1}{4q})
.
Following
31
, we utilize an infinitely differentiable bump function constructed via:

u_{1}(x)=\begin{cases}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{2}-\bm{x})(\bm{x}-\frac{1}{4})}\right),&\bm{x}\in(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2})\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and define
u:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
through the normalization:

u(\bm{x})=\left(\int_{1/4}^{1/2}u_{1}(t)\,dt\right)^{-1}\int_{\bm{x}}^{\infty}u_{1}(t)\,dt.

Let
C_{\phi}\in(0,\delta_{0}]
and function
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be defined as

\phi(\bm{x})=C_{\phi}u(\|\bm{x}\|).

Let
\varphi_{j}(\bm{x})=q^{-\beta}\phi\left(q(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{j})\right)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{j}}(\bm{x})
for
j=1,\ldots,m
.
We define the expected reward function for arm 2 as
\eta_{2}\equiv\frac{1}{2}
, while
\eta_{1,i}:[0,1]^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
represents the expected reward function for arm 1 with

\eta_{1,i}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{2}+\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{m}\varphi_{j}(\bm{x}).

Hence, there are
m
possible configurations of arm 1. The constraint
C_{\phi}\leq\delta_{0}
ensures
\eta_{1,i}(\bm{x})\in[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}]\subset[0,1]
.

When
K=2
, we need to check that the constructed instances satisfy Assumptions
4
-
8
. First, Assumptions
4
-
5
can be verified directly following the original steps in
31
, thus we omit it for simplicity. Assumption
6
can be also verified similarly as in
31
by noting that
\operatorname{Leb}[\mathcal{X}_{0}]=(1-O(q^{-\alpha\beta}))\operatorname{Leb}([0,1]^{d})=(1-o(1))\operatorname{Leb}([0,1]^{d})
for sufficiently large
T
.

•

Assumption
7
. Define the reference point as
\bm{x}_{0}=\left(\frac{1}{2q},\ldots,\frac{1}{2q}\right)
. The margin probability can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(0<\left|\eta_{1,i}(X)-\frac{1}{2}\right|\leq t\right)

\displaystyle=(m-1)\mathbb{P}_{\sigma}\left(0<\phi\left[q\left(X-\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right]\leq tq^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=(m-1)\int_{\mathcal{B}\left(\bm{x}_{0},\frac{1}{4q}\right)}\mathbb{I}\left\{0<\phi\left[q\left(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right]\leq tq^{\beta}\right\}\frac{\omega}{\operatorname{Leb}\left[\mathcal{B}\left(0,\frac{1}{4q}\right)\right]}d\bm{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{(m-1)\omega}{\operatorname{Leb}\left[\mathcal{B}\left(0,\frac{1}{4}\right)\right]}\int_{\mathcal{B}\left(0,\frac{1}{4}\right)}\mathbb{I}\left\{\phi(y)\leq tq^{\beta}\right\}dy

\displaystyle=(m-1)\omega\cdot\mathbb{I}\left\{t\geq C_{\phi}q^{-\beta}\right\}.

Since
(m-1)\omega\leq 2q^{-\alpha\beta}
, Assumption
7
is satisfied with
C_{0}=2C_{\phi}^{-\alpha}
.

•

Assumption
8
.
Since
\max_{i,j\in\mathcal{K}}\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\Delta_{i,j}(\bm{x})\leq C_{\phi}\left(4C_{\phi}\frac{T}{C}\right)^{-\frac{\beta}{\beta+d}}\leq T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}}CT^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}
holds by choosing a sufficiently small
C_{\phi}
, Assumption
8
automatically holds.

According to Definition
1
, since all candidate algorithms are
(1-\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{T}))
-fair algorithms and
\eta_{1,l}(\bm{x})=\eta_{2}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{2}
for
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{l}
, we have that, with probability at least
1-\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{T})
, for all
t\in[T]
and
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{l}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=1|\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x},\mathcal{F}_{l,t-1})=\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=2|\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x},\mathcal{F}_{l,t-1}),

(A.47)

where
\mathcal{F}_{l,t-1}
is the filtration at time
t-1
generated by reward function
\eta_{1,l}(\bm{x})
.

At time
t
, the corruption term
c_{t}
is defined as

\displaystyle c_{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\eta_{1,i}(\bm{x}_{t})-\frac{1}{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\varphi_{j}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mathbb{I}(\pi_{t}=1)=-\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}(\pi_{t}=1),

with
i\in\{1,...,m\}
. It follows that

\displaystyle|c_{t}|\leq|\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})|=q^{-\beta}\phi\left(q(\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{i})\right)\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\leq q^{-\beta}C_{\phi}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t}).

Under the event that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\leq 2Tm\omega
, the average total corruption consumption (ignoring the budget for a moment) among
m
instances can be bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{t=1}^{T}|\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}C_{\phi}q^{-\beta}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}C_{\phi}q^{-\beta}2Tm\omega

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{\phi}q^{-d-\beta}T\leq 2C_{\phi}\frac{1}{4C_{\phi}}\frac{C}{T}T=\frac{C}{2}.

Thus, among all
m
instances, for at most
\lceil\frac{m}{2}\rceil
instances, the corruption consumption for the total horizon is greater than
C
, which implies that at least
\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor
instances satisfy
\sum_{t=1}^{T}|c_{t}|\leq C
. Denote the set of indices of these instances as
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,m\}
, which consists of at least
\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor
elements.

Under the event that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\leq 2Tm\omega
, for all
i\in\mathcal{I}
, the corrupted expected reward function for arm 1 is
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\varphi_{j}(\bm{x}).
Thus, any admissible algorithm can not distinguish among all
i\in\mathcal{I}
during the whole time period
t\in[T]
, i.e., at time
t
, the distribution of
\mathcal{F}_{i,t-1}
is the same for all
i\in\mathcal{I}
. Hence, for any
i,j\in\mathcal{I}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=1|\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x},\mathcal{F}_{i,t-1})=\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=1|\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x},\mathcal{F}_{j,t-1})

(A.48)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=2|\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x},\mathcal{F}_{i,t-1})=\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=2|\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x},\mathcal{F}_{j,t-1}).

(A.49)

Under the event
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\leq 2Tm\omega
, in order to maintain the fairness, (
A.47
) holds for all
l\in\mathcal{I}
, which, by combining (
A.48
) and (
A.49
), yields for all
t\in[T]
and
\bm{x}\in\cup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{X}_{i}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=1|\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x},\mathcal{F}_{l,t-1})=\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=2|\bm{x}_{t}=\bm{x},\mathcal{F}_{l,t-1}).

Without loss of generality, we assume
1\in\mathcal{I}
. For the ease of nationality, we denote event
\mathcal{A}
as
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\leq 2Tm\omega
,
\mathcal{B}
as (
A.47
) holds for all
l\in\mathcal{I}
, and
\mathcal{C}
as
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus 1}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\geq\frac{1}{2}T(\frac{1}{2}m-1)\omega
.

Next, we consider bounding the probability of
\mathcal{A}^{C}
,
\mathcal{B}^{C}
, and
\mathcal{C}^{C}
. By the definition of
(1-\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{T}))
-fairness,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{C})=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T}\right).

(A.50)

Since
\bm{x}_{1},...,\bm{x}_{T}
are i.i.d. distributed, we have
\mathbb{E}[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})]=Tm\omega
. By Cantelli’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{C})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\geq 2Tm\omega\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})-\mathbb{E}[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})]\geq Tm\omega\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1-m\omega}{(1-m\omega)+Tm\omega}\leq\frac{1}{1+Tm\omega},

(A.51)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{C}\cap\mathcal{A})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2}T(\frac{1}{2}m-1)\omega\right\}\bigcap\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2}T(\frac{1}{2}m-1)\omega\right\}\bigcap\mathcal{A}\bigcap\left\{|\mathcal{I}|\geq\frac{m}{2}-1\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\leq\frac{1}{2}T(\frac{1}{2}m-1)\omega\right\}\bigcap\left\{|\mathcal{I}|\geq\frac{m}{2}-1\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\frac{1}{2}T(\frac{1}{2}m-1)\omega}\leq\frac{8}{Tm\omega+8}.

(A.52)

By (
A.4.11
) and (
A.4.11
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{C})=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{C}\cap\mathcal{A})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{C}\cap\mathcal{A}^{C})\leq\frac{8}{Tm\omega+8}+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{C})\leq\frac{9}{Tm\omega+1}.

(A.53)

When
\pi_{t}=2
and
\bm{x}_{t}\in\cup_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus{1}}\mathcal{X}_{i}
, it holds that

\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})=\eta_{1,1}(\bm{x}_{t})-\frac{1}{2}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus{1}}\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t}).

Conditional on
\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}_{i}
, since
\bm{x}_{t}
is uniformly distributed in
\mathcal{B}(x_{i},\frac{1}{4q})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})|\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}_{i})\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}\left(x_{0},\frac{1}{4q}\right)}q^{-\beta}\phi\left[q\left(x-x_{0}\right)\right]\frac{\omega}{\operatorname{Leb}\left[\mathcal{B}\left(0,\frac{1}{4q}\right)\right]}\left(\frac{\omega}{\operatorname{Leb}\left[\mathcal{X}_{0}\cup\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{X}_{i}\right)\right]}\right)^{-1}dx

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{Leb}\left[\mathcal{X}_{0}\cup\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{X}_{i}\right)\right]}{\operatorname{Leb}\left[\mathcal{B}\left(0,\frac{1}{4}\right)\right]}q^{-\beta}\int_{\mathcal{B}\left(0,\frac{1}{4}\right)}\phi(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{\phi}q^{-\beta},

(A.54)

where the last inequality is because
\operatorname{Leb}\left[\mathcal{X}_{0}\cup\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{X}_{i}\right)\right]
is lower bounded by
\frac{1}{2}
for sufficiently large
T
, and
\int_{\mathcal{B}\left(0,\frac{1}{4}\right)}\phi(y)dy
is a constant.
Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})|\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})|\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}_{i},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})|\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}_{i},\mathcal{A}^{C}\cup\mathcal{B}^{C}\cup\mathcal{C}^{C})\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{C}\cup\mathcal{B}^{C}\cup\mathcal{C}^{C}),

(A.55)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})|\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}_{i},\mathcal{A}^{C}\cup\mathcal{B}^{C}\cup\mathcal{C}^{C})\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{C}\cup\mathcal{B}^{C}\cup\mathcal{C}^{C})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\phi}q^{-\beta}\left(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{C})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{C})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{C})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\phi}q^{-\beta}\left(\frac{10}{Tm\omega+1}+\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{m}{T}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\phi}}{2}q^{-\beta},

(A.56)

when
T
is sufficiently large, where the first inequality is by the union bound, the second inequality is by (
A.50
), (
A.4.11
), and (
A.53
), and the last inequality is because
m=O(T)
and
Tm\omega
goes to infinity as
T
goes to infinity.

Combining (
A.4.11
)-(
A.4.11
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})|\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}_{i},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})\geq\frac{c_{\phi}}{2}q^{-\beta}.

(A.57)

The event
\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{B}
implies that for all
t\in[T]
,
\mathbb{P}(\pi_{t}=2|\bm{x}_{t}\in\cup_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus{1}}\mathcal{X}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{1,t-1})=\frac{1}{2}
.
Therefore, by (
A.4.11
), the regret can be lower bounded by

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\biggr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\max_{k}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{t})-f^{*}_{\pi_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t})\right)\mathbb{I}{(\pi_{t}=2)}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus 1}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\bigg|\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\biggr]\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus{1}}\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}(\pi_{t}=2)\bigg|\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\right]\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi_{i}(\bm{x}_{t})\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\bigg|\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}_{i},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\right]\mathbb{P}(\bm{x}_{t}\in\mathcal{X}_{i}|\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})\bigg|\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\right]\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}c_{\phi}q^{-\beta}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus 1}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{t})\bigg|\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\biggr]\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}c_{\phi}q^{-\beta}T(\frac{1}{2}m-1)\omega\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}c_{\phi}q^{-\beta}T(\frac{1}{2}m-1)\left(1-(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{C})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{C})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{C}))\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{8}c_{\phi}q^{-\beta}T(\frac{1}{2}m-1),

where the last inequality holds for sufficiently large
T
by the calculation in (
A.4.11
). Therefore, we have

\displaystyle R_{T}={\Omega}(q^{-\beta}T(\frac{1}{2}m-1)\omega)={\Omega}(C^{\frac{\alpha\beta+\beta}{\beta+d}}T^{\frac{d-\alpha\beta}{\beta+d}}),

which, together with Proposition
A.9
, implies

\displaystyle R_{T}={\Omega}\left(T^{\frac{\beta+d-\alpha\beta}{2\beta+d}}+C^{\frac{\alpha\beta+\beta}{\beta+d}}T^{\frac{d-\alpha\beta}{\beta+d}}\right).

This finishes the proof.
\square

A.5
Proofs in Section
A.1

The following lemmas will be used in this subsection. Lemma
A.13
directly follows from the Bernstein concentration inequality for martingale difference sequences, which also serves as an intermediate result in the proof of Proposition EC.1 in
8
. Lemma
A.14
is a consequence of combining Lemmas EC.22 and EC.23 in
8
.
Lemma
A.15
provides a uniform probability bound on the inner product between the estimation error and any vector within a given ball.

Lemma A.13
.

Let
{Z_{s}:s\in\mathcal{J}}
be a collection of random vectors and assume that each element in
Z_{s}
lies in
[-r,r]
.
Assume entries of the random noise vector
\bm{\epsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{J}|}
are independent
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian random variables. If
|\mathcal{J}|
is a fixed positive integer, then for all constants
\widetilde{\chi}>0
and
\phi>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k}\|_{2}\geq\widetilde{\chi})\leq\exp{(-\widetilde{C}|\mathcal{J}|\widetilde{\chi}^{2}+\log 2d)}+\mathbb{P}(\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{J}))\leq\phi^{2}),

where
\widetilde{C}=\frac{\phi^{4}}{2dr^{2}\sigma^{2}}
and
\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{J})=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{J}|}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{J}}Z_{s}Z_{s}^{\mathrm{T}}
.

Lemma A.14
.

Assume the conditions in Lemma
A.13
hold. For a subset
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{J}
such that
\{\bm{z}_{t}|t\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\}
is an i.i.d. sample drawn from a distribution
\mathsf{P}_{Z}
, with
\lambda_{\min}(E_{\bm{z}\sim\mathsf{P}_{Z}}[\bm{z}\bm{z}^{\mathcal{T}}])\geq\phi_{1}^{2}
and
|\mathcal{J}^{\prime}|/|\mathcal{J}|\geq p/2
for positive constants
\phi_{1}
and
p
, if
|\mathcal{J}|
is a fixed positive integer, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{J}))\leq\frac{\phi_{1}^{2}p}{4}\right]\leq\exp\left[-p\widetilde{C}\left(\phi_{1}\right)|\mathcal{J}|/2+\log d\right],

where
\widetilde{C}\left(\phi_{1}\right):=\min\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{\phi_{1}^{2}}{8r^{2}}\right)
.

Lemma A.15
.

Assume that each element in
Z
lies in
[-r,r]
and that each entry of the random noise vector
\bm{\epsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{J}|}
is independent
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian random variables. If
|\mathcal{J}|
is a fixed positive integer, then for all constants
\chi>0
,
\phi>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\|a\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{dr^{2}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}a|\geq\chi)\leq\exp{(-D_{3}|\mathcal{J}|\chi^{2}+\log 2d)}+\mathbb{P}(\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{J}))\leq\phi^{2}),

where
D_{3}=\frac{\phi^{4}}{2d^{2}r^{4}\sigma^{2}}
and
\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{J})=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{J}|}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{J}}Z_{s}Z_{s}^{\mathrm{T}}
.

Proof.

Proof of Lemma
A.15
.
By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, for any
a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, it holds that

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}a|\leq\|\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k}\|_{2}\|a\|_{2},

which leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\|a\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{dr^{2}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}a|\geq\chi)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k}\|_{2}\max_{\|a\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{dr^{2}}}\|a\|_{2}\geq\chi)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k}\|_{2}\geq\chi/\max_{\|a\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{dr^{2}}}\|a\|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k}\|_{2}\geq\chi/\sqrt{dr^{2}}).

(A.58)

According to Lemma
A.13
, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k}\|_{2}\geq\chi/\sqrt{dr^{2}})\leq\exp{\left(-\frac{\widetilde{C}}{dr^{2}}|\mathcal{J}|{\chi}^{2}+\log 2d\right)}+\mathbb{P}(\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{J}))\leq\phi^{2}).

Therefore, by using (
A.5
) and replacing
\frac{\widetilde{C}}{dr^{2}}
with
D_{3}
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\|a\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{dr^{2}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{J})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}a|\geq\chi)

\displaystyle\leq\exp{(-D_{3}|\mathcal{J}|{\chi}^{2}+\log 2d)}+\mathbb{P}(\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{J}))\leq\phi^{2}),

which finishes the proof.
\square

A.5.1
Proof of Proposition
A.1

Choose an arm from the arm set
{\mathcal{K}}
, and without loss of generality denote it as arm
1
in this proof. Consider the index set
\mathcal{I}_{k,0}^{\prime}=\{t:\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{1},t\in\mathcal{I}_{k,0}\}
, where
Q_{1}
is as in Assumption
3
. First, it is obvious that the random variables
\{\bm{z}_{t}:t\in\mathcal{I}_{k,0}^{\prime}\}
are i.i.d. distributed sample drawn from
\mathbb{P}_{Z|Z\in Q_{1}}
with length

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}^{\prime}|=\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{0}}\mathbb{I}(\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{1})\mathbb{I}(t\in\mathcal{I}_{k,0}).

By Assumption
3
and the random exploration execution of Algorithm
1
before
|\mathcal{T}_{0}|
, it holds that
\mathbb{P}(\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{1},t\in\mathcal{I}_{k,0})\geq\widetilde{p}/K
for all
t\in\mathcal{T}_{0}
. Then we can obtain that
\mathbb{E}(|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}^{\prime}|)\geq|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\widetilde{p}/K
. Using Hoeffding’s inequality, the size of
\mathcal{I}_{k,0}^{\prime}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}^{\prime}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2K}\left|\mathcal{T}_{0}\right|\right]\leq e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{2K^{2}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{0}\right|}.

Since
\mathcal{I}_{k,0}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{0}
, we can infer that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}^{\prime}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2K}\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}\right|\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}^{\prime}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2K}\left|\mathcal{T}_{0}\right|\right]\leq e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{2K^{2}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{0}\right|}.

As stated in Assumption
3
, we have that
\min_{i\in\mathcal{K}}\lambda_{\min}\{\mathbb{E}(\bm{z}_{t}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}|\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{i})\}\geq\lambda^{*}
, which implies
\lambda_{\min}\{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{z}\sim\mathbb{P}_{Z|Z\in Q_{1}}}(\bm{z}\bm{z}^{\mathrm{T}})\}\geq\lambda^{*}
. Define the event
\mathcal{A}=\{|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|\geq\frac{1}{2K}|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\}
. Then according to Lemma
A.14
, when letting
\mathcal{J}=\mathcal{I}_{k,0}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}=\mathcal{I}_{k,0}^{\prime}
,
p=\widetilde{p}/K
and
\phi_{1}^{2}=\lambda^{*}
,
it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0}))\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}{4K}\mid\mathcal{A}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0}))\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}{4K}\mid|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|=n,n\geq\frac{1}{2K}|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left[-\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2K^{2}}\widetilde{C}\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{*}}\right)|\mathcal{T}_{0}|/2+\log d\right]+\exp\left[{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{2K^{2}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{0}\right|}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2T^{-4},

(A.59)

where the last inequality is by the assumptions on the parameters stated in Proposition
A.1
.

Under event
\mathcal{A}
, by plugging (
A.5.1
) into Lemma
A.15
, we have that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\chi)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\frac{D_{1}}{2K}|\mathcal{T}_{0}|{\chi}^{2}+\log 2d)}+\mathbb{P}(\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0}))\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}{4K})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\frac{D_{1}}{2K}C_{a}\log T{\chi}^{2}+\log 2d)}+2T^{-4}

(A.60)

where
D_{1}=\frac{\lambda^{*2}\widetilde{p}^{2}}{32d^{2}r^{4}\sigma^{2}K^{2}}
.
By letting
\chi=\frac{h}{4}
, it follows that, under event
\mathcal{A}
, with the assumptions on
C_{a}
, it can be verified that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{h}{4})\leq 3T^{-4}.

Recall that for all
t\in\mathcal{T}_{0}
, the algorithm randomly pull arms. Thus, using the
Hoeffding’s inequality again,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{A}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-\frac{1}{2K^{2}}\left|\mathcal{T}_{0}\right|}\geq 1-\frac{1}{T^{4}}.

(A.61)

By the union bound, we have for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{h}{4})\leq 4T^{-4},

which further implies that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{h}{4})\leq 4KT^{-4}.

This finishes the proof.
\square

A.5.2
Proof of Proposition
A.2

Define the index set
\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}=\{u:\bm{z}_{u}\in Q_{k},|\mathcal{T}_{0}|<u\leq t\}
, then we have the random variables
\{\bm{z}_{u}:u\in\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\}
consists of i.i.d. random variables with distribution
\mathbb{P}_{Z|Z\in Q_{k}}
. It follows that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}|=\sum_{u=\mathcal{T}_{0}+1}^{t}\mathbb{I}(\bm{z}_{u}\in Q_{k}).

Since
\mathbb{P}(\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{k})\geq\widetilde{p}
, by Hoeffding’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|)\right]\leq e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|)},

(A.62)

which, together with the fact that
\mathcal{I}_{k,t}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{k,0}\subseteq[t]\setminus\mathcal{T}_{0}
, implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2}(\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}\right|-\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}\right|)\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\right|<\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|)\right]\leq e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|)}.

Define event
\mathcal{B}=\{\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|<\frac{h}{4}\}
. Denote
l=\arg\max_{j\in\mathcal{K}\setminus\{k\}}\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}_{j,0}
. Then under event
\mathcal{B}
, if
\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{k}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}_{k,0}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}_{l,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}_{k,0}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}{\beta}_{k}+\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}{\beta}_{k}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{l}+\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\beta_{l}-\bm{z}_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}\widehat{\beta}_{l,0}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{h}{4}+h-\frac{h}{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{h}{2}.

Therefore, under event
\mathcal{B}
, for all
t>\mathcal{T}_{0}
, the algorithm would pull arm
k
if
\bm{z}_{t}\in Q_{k}
, which implies

\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{k,t}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{k,0}
. Hence, by (
A.62
), we can obtain that under event
\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}\right|-\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|)\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|)\right]\leq e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|)}.

(A.63)

Assume
t\geq 2|\mathcal{T}_{0}|+1
, which gives
t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|\geq\frac{t+1}{2}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}(t+1)\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\right|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|)\right]\leq e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{2}(t-|\mathcal{T}_{0}|)}\leq e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{4}(t+1)},

which also implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}\right|/|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|\leq\frac{\widetilde{p}}{4}\right]\leq e^{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{4}(t+1)}.

According to Lemma
A.14
, when letting
\mathcal{J}=\mathcal{I}_{k,t}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}=\mathcal{I}_{k,t}^{\prime}
,
p=\widetilde{p}/2
and
\phi_{1}^{2}=\lambda^{*}
, it follows that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t}))\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}\widetilde{p}}{8}\mid\mathcal{B}\right]\leq\exp\left[-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{8}\widetilde{C}\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{*}}\right)t+\log d\right]+\exp\left[-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{4}t\right],

(A.64)

where the first inequality is because
\mathcal{I}_{k,t}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{k,0}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{k,t}
.

By Lemma
A.15
, together with (
A.63
), letting
\widetilde{\chi}=\frac{C_{b}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{t+1}}
and
D_{4}=\frac{\lambda^{*2}\widetilde{p}^{2}}{512d^{2}r^{4}\sigma^{2}}
, we have that under event
\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{C_{b}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{t+1}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\frac{1}{2}C_{b}^{2}D_{4}\widetilde{p}\frac{t+1}{t+1}\log T+\log 2d)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\exp\left[-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{8}\widetilde{C}\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{*}}\right)t+\log d\right]+2\exp\left[{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{4}t}\right].

Then taking union bound with respect to all arms
k\in{\mathcal{K}}
and combining with the fact that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B})\geq 1-4KT^{-4}
by Proposition
A.1
, it further yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{t+1}}{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\frac{1}{2}C_{b}^{2}D_{4}\widetilde{p}\log T+\log 2d+\log K)}+\exp\left[-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{8}\widetilde{C}\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{*}}\right)t+\log d+\log K\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\exp\left[{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{4}t}+\log 2K\right]+4KT^{-4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left[-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{8}\widetilde{C}\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{*}}\right)t+\log d+\log K\right]+\exp\left[{-\frac{\widetilde{p}^{2}}{4}t}+\log 2K\right]+5KT^{-4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8KT^{-4},

(A.65)

where the second inequality is obtained by the assumption
T>2d
and
C_{b}>\sqrt{\frac{10}{D_{4}\widetilde{p}}}
, and the third inequality is verified by the assumption
C_{a}>\frac{20}{\widetilde{p}D_{2}}\vee\frac{8}{\widetilde{p}^{2}}
.

When
|\mathcal{T}_{0}|<t\leq 2|\mathcal{T}_{0}|
, since
\mathcal{I}_{k,0}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{k,t}
, it is obvious that
|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|\leq|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|
and
|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0}))\leq|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t}))
, which leads to

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{I}_{k,t}|\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t}))}\|Z(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})^{T}\bm{\epsilon}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{I}_{k,0}|\lambda_{\min}(\widehat{\Sigma}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0}))}\|Z(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})^{T}\bm{\epsilon}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})\|_{2}.

Then as shown in the proof of Lemma
A.13
, we have that
\|\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k}\|_{2}
shares the same tail bound as of
\|\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,0})-\beta_{k}\|_{2}
given in Lemma
A.13
. Furthermore, by taking similar steps as in the proof of Proposition
A.1
, which is omitted for simplicity, we can guarantee

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{h}{4})\leq 4KT^{-4}.

(A.66)

Therefore, when
|\mathcal{T}_{0}|<t\leq 2|\mathcal{T}_{0}|
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{t+1}}{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{C_{b}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{t+1}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{C_{b}}{2\sqrt{2C_{a}+1}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{h}{4})

\displaystyle\leq 4KT^{-4},

(A.67)

where the first inequality is because
t\leq 2|\mathcal{T}_{0}|
and the second inequality is because
C_{b}\geq\frac{h}{2}\sqrt{2C_{a}+1}
, and the last inequality is given by (
A.66
).

Combining (
A.5.2
) and (
A.5.2
), we have that, for all
t>|\mathcal{T}_{0}|
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}|(\widehat{\beta}_{k}(\mathcal{I}_{k,t})-\beta_{k})^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{z}|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{t+1}}{2})\leq 8KT^{-4}.

\square

A.6
Proofs in Section
A.2

A.6.1
Proof of Lemma
A.4

Consider an arm
k\in\mathcal{K}
. Let
h(\bm{x})=\max_{j\in\mathcal{K}}f_{j}^{*}(\bm{x})-f_{k}^{*}(\bm{x})
, where we suppress the dependency on
k
for the ease of notation. By the continuity of max function, we have
h(\bm{x})
is continuous.
We prove Lemma
A.4
by contradiction.

Suppose Lemma
A.4
does not hold, that is, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
\bm{s}\in\mathcal{X}\setminus(B(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(k)})\cup\mathcal{R}_{k})
, s.t.
0<h(\bm{s})\leq\epsilon
. Let
\epsilon_{n}=1/n
,
n=1,2,...
. Then for every
\epsilon_{n}
, there exists
\bm{x}_{n}\in\mathcal{X}\setminus(B(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(k)})\cup\mathcal{R}_{k})
such that
0<h(\bm{x}_{n})\leq\epsilon_{n}
. By the Bolzano–Weierstrass theorem, since
X_{n}=\{\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2},...\}
are on a bounded set, there exists a convergent subsequence
X_{n}^{\prime}=\{\bm{x}_{n_{1}},\bm{x}_{n_{2}},...\}
. Let

\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\bm{x}_{n_{l}}=\bm{x}_{0}.

Hence, by the continuity of
h(\bm{x})
, we have

0\leq h(\bm{x}_{0})=\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}h(\bm{x}_{n_{l}})\leq\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\epsilon_{n_{l}}=0,

which implies
\bm{x}_{0}\in\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(k)}\cup\mathcal{R}_{k}
.
Since
\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\bm{x}_{n_{l}}=\bm{x}_{0}
, we have
\|\bm{x}_{n_{g}}-\bm{x}_{0}\|_{2}\leq\mathfrak{r}
for some large enough
n_{g}
, which contradicts the fact that
\bm{x}_{n_{g}}\in\mathcal{X}\setminus(B(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(k)})\cup\mathcal{R}_{k})
. Thus, there exists a constant
c^{\prime\prime}>0
such that for all
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}\setminus(B(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(k)})\cup\mathcal{R}_{k})
,
h(\bm{x})>c^{\prime\prime}
, which concludes the proof.
\square

A.6.2
Proof of Lemma
A.5

By the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{k}
, if
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{R}_{k}
, we have for any
j\in\mathcal{K}
,

\displaystyle f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})-f_{j}^{*}(\bm{x})\geq 0.

We prove that
k\in\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}
, which implies
{\mathcal{R}}_{k}\subseteq S_{k,q}
, by induction. First, obviously
k\in\mathcal{K}_{1,u(\bm{x})}
. For
h\leq q-1
, assume
k\in\mathcal{K}_{h,u(\bm{x})}
, which implies that
\bm{x}\in S_{h,k}
. Then denote
b_{h}=\arg\max_{j\in\mathcal{K}_{h,u(\bm{x})}}\widehat{f}_{h,j}(\bm{x})
, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{h,b_{h}}(\bm{x})-\widehat{f}_{h,k}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\widehat{f}_{h,b_{h}}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{b_{h}}(\bm{x}))+f^{*}_{b_{h}}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})-(\widehat{f}_{h,k}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}))

\displaystyle\leq\left|\widehat{f}_{h,b_{h}}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{b_{h}}(\bm{x})\right|+f^{*}_{b_{h}}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})+\left|\widehat{f}_{h,k}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{h}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{h}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{h},

where the second inequality is obtained by the definition of
{\mathcal{R}}_{k}
and event
\mathcal{G}_{h}
. Thus,
\widehat{f}_{h,k}(G_{u(\bm{x})})
and
\max\limits_{l\in\mathcal{K}_{h,u(\bm{x})}}\widehat{f}_{h,l}(G_{u(\bm{x})})
are
(2\epsilon_{h})
-chained in
\{\widehat{f}_{h,k}(G_{u(\bm{x})}):k\in\mathcal{K}_{h,u(\bm{x})}\}
, which implies that
k\in\mathcal{K}_{h+1,u(\bm{x})}
. Therefore, with the induction assumption we have
k\in\mathcal{K}_{h+1,u(\bm{x})}
, from which we can conclude that
k\in\mathcal{K}_{q,u(\bm{x})}
.
\square

A.6.3
Proof of Lemma
A.6

Note that
Q
is the smallest integer such that
\sum_{q=1}^{Q}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|\geq T
. By the definition of
|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|
, for any positive integer
Q_{0}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q_{0}}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{q=1}^{Q_{0}}\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{4^{q}\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{q=1}^{Q_{0}}\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{\log T}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}2^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta}q}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2K}{p^{*}}(2^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta}Q_{0}})\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}.

Let
Q_{0}=\lceil\frac{\beta}{(2\beta+d)\log 2}\log(\frac{Tp^{*}}{2K}\left(\log T\right)^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}+\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}})\rceil
, we have
\sum_{q=1}^{Q_{0}}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|\geq T
, which leads to that
Q\leq Q_{0}\leq\lceil\frac{\beta}{(2\beta+d)\log 2}\log(T\left(\log T\right)^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}+\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}})\rceil
. It follows that

\displaystyle\epsilon_{q}\geq\epsilon_{Q}=2^{-Q}(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\geq\frac{1}{2}T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{A}.

\square

A.6.4
Proof of Lemma
A.7

By Lemma
A.6
, we have that for all
q\leq Q
,
\epsilon_{q-1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{Q}\geq T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}
. Thus, for any arm
i\in\mathcal{K}
and
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}
, let
j=\arg\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{j,q-1}(\bm{x})-\widehat{f}_{i,q-1}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\widehat{f}_{j,q-1}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x}))+f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x})-(\widehat{f}_{i,q-1}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}))

\displaystyle\leq 1/2\epsilon_{q-1}+\epsilon_{q-1}+1/2\epsilon_{q-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon_{q-1},

which implies that
\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}
. Thus,
S_{q,k}=\mathcal{X}
for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
.
\square

A.6.5
Proof of Lemma
A.8

The proof of this lemma follows a similar structure to Lemma 16 in
31
, except that we need to prove the inequality
\epsilon_{q}\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{A}
, which is given in Lemma
A.6
. Then we can directly revise the proof steps from Lemma 16 of
31
to obtain our final conclusion.

\square

A.6.6
Proof of Proposition
A.9

Since Assumptions
4
-
7
are exactly the same to those in
31
, we have that the problem instance given in Theorem 3 in
31
satisfies Assumptions
4
-
7
. It remains to check that the problem instance satisfies Assumption
8
. We describe the features of problem class following the notations used in
31
. There are two arms called arm 1 and -1, and fix
\delta_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
such that
\max_{i,j\in\mathcal{K}}\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\Delta_{i,j}(\bm{x})\leq C_{\phi}\left(\frac{T}{4e(\frac{1}{4}-\delta_{0}^{2})}\right)^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\leq T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}
. Therefore, Assumption
8
automatically holds.
\square

A.7
Proofs in Section
A.4

A.7.1
Proof of Lemma
A.10

Note that
Q^{RS}
is the smallest integer such that
\sum_{q=1}^{Q^{RS}}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|\geq T
. By the definition of
|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|
,

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{Q_{0}}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{q=1}^{Q_{0}}\left(\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{(C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q})\log\left(T\delta_{A}^{-d}\right)}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{q=1}^{Q_{0}}\left(\frac{2K}{p^{*}}\left(\frac{\log T}{C_{K}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}(2^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta}q}\vee C^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2K}{p^{*}}(2^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta}Q_{0}}\vee(C^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}))\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}.

Let
Q_{0}=\lceil\frac{\beta}{(2\beta+d)\log 2}\log(\frac{Tp^{*}}{2K}\left(\log T\right)^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}+\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}})\rceil\wedge\lceil\frac{Tp^{*}}{2K}\left(\log T\right)^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}+\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}C^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}\rceil
, and thus
\sum_{q=1}^{Q_{0}}|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|\geq T
, which leads to that
Q^{RS}\leq Q_{0}\leq\lceil\frac{\beta}{(2\beta+d)\log 2}\log(\frac{Tp^{*}}{2K}\left(\log T\right)^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}+\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}})\rceil\wedge\lceil\frac{Tp^{*}}{2K}\left(\log T\right)^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}+\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}C^{-\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta}}\rceil
. It follows that

\displaystyle\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}\geq\epsilon^{\prime}_{Q^{RS}}

\displaystyle=(2^{-Q^{RS}}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\vee T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}

\displaystyle\geq T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\geq(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})\delta_{A},

where the last inequality is because
\frac{1}{1+L_{1}\sqrt{d}}\geq\frac{1}{\log T}
.
\square

A.7.2
Proof of Lemma
A.11

By direct computation, we have that for all
q\leq Q^{RS}
,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS}

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon_{Q^{RS}}^{RS}=\left(2^{-Q^{RS}}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\vee T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\right)+\frac{2\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}}C(\frac{p^{*}}{4K}|{\mathcal{T}}_{Q^{RS}}^{RS}|)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}

\displaystyle\geq T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}}CT^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}},

where the last inequality is by
\frac{p^{*}}{4K}|{\mathcal{T}}_{Q^{RS}}^{RS}|\leq T
.
Thus, for any arm
i\in\mathcal{K}
and
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}
, let
j=\arg\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}}f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{f}^{RS}_{j,q-1}(\bm{x})-\widehat{f}^{RS}_{i,q-1}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(\widehat{f}^{RS}_{j,q-1}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x}))+f^{*}_{j}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x})-(\widehat{f}^{RS}_{i,q-1}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{i}(\bm{x}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS}+\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS}

\displaystyle=2\epsilon_{q-1}^{RS},

which implies that
\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}
. Thus,
S_{q,k}^{RS}=\mathcal{X}
for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
.
\square

A.7.3
Proof of Lemma
A.12

Recall that the estimator
\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}
is trained based on corrupted samples
\mathcal{T}_{q,k}=\left\{\left(\bm{x}_{t},\widetilde{y}_{t}\right):t\in\mathcal{T}_{q},\,\mathcal{\pi}_{t}=k\right\}
, with
\widetilde{y}_{t}=y_{t}+c_{t}
.
Define
h_{q,k}=n_{q,k}^{-1/(2\beta+d)}
. Let

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)=\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q,k}}W\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right)U\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right)U^{T}\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right),

where
W(\bm{x})=\mathbb{I}(\|\bm{x}\|_{2}\leq 1)
,
U(u)=\left(u^{r}\right)_{|r|\leq\beta}
is a vector-valued function from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
\mathbb{R}^{M_{\beta}}
. Denote
f_{k,\mathfrak{b}(\beta)}\left(\bm{x};\bm{x}_{0}\right)=\sum_{|r|\leq\mathfrak{b}(\beta)}\frac{\left(\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{0}\right)^{r}}{r!}D^{r}f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)
. Then fix
\bm{x}_{0}\in G\cap S_{q,k}^{RS}
, and by the estimation error decomposition given in Step I in the proof of Lemma 16 in
31
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x}_{0})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{0})\right|\leq\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)}\left(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\right),

(A.68)

where

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q,k}}\left(\widetilde{y}_{t}-f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\right)W\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q,k}}\left(f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)-f_{k,\mathfrak{b}(\beta)}\left(\bm{x}_{t};\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)W\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right).

We decompose
\Gamma_{1}
into the following two components:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q,k}}\left(\widetilde{y}_{t}-f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right)\right)W\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q,k}}(y_{t}-f_{k}^{*}\left(\bm{x}_{t}\right))W\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right)+\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q,k}}c_{t}W\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{1,1}+\Gamma_{1,2}.

Following the proof of Theorem 5 in
31
, denote event
\mathcal{E}_{q,k}(\bm{x}_{0}):=\{\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k},\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)\geq\lambda_{0}\}
, then
it can be established that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)}(\Gamma_{1,1}+\Gamma_{2})\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}\bigg|\mathcal{E}_{q,k}(\bm{x}_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime 2}\right),

(A.69)

where
\lambda_{0}
is as in Lemma
A.8
.

Then we focus on the term
\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)}\Gamma_{1,2}
. Direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1,2}=\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q,k}}c_{t}W\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{q,k}}c_{t}\leq\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C,

where the first inequality is because
W\left(\frac{\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{0}}{h_{q,k}}\right)\leq 1
, and the last inequality is because the total corruption budget is upper bounded by
C
. Consequently, under the event that
\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)\geq\lambda_{0}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)}\Gamma_{1,2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}}\frac{1}{n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C.

(A.70)

Combining (
A.7.3
) and (
A.70
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})}{\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)}\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C\;\bigg|\;\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},

\displaystyle\quad N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k},\;\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)\geq\lambda_{0}\bigg)\leq 4\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime 2}\right).

According to the Lemma 10 in
31
, under event
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS}
and
N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k}
, with probability at least
1-2M_{\beta}^{2}\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\right)
, the minimum eigenvalue of
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)
satisfies
\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)\geq\lambda_{0}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{\min}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{q,k}\left(\bm{x}_{0}\right)\right)}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(4+2M_{\beta}^{2})\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime 2}\right),

which, by (
A.68
), implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x}_{0})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x}_{0})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(4+2M_{\beta}^{2})\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime 2}\right).

Taking union bound over all
\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\cap G
gives that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\cap G}\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{A}^{-d}(4+2M_{\beta}^{2})\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime 2}\right).

(A.71)

The Lipschitz condition of
f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})
allows us to conclude that

\displaystyle|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})|

\displaystyle=\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(g(\bm{x}))-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(g(\bm{x}))-f^{*}_{k}(g(\bm{x}))\right|+\left|f^{*}_{k}(g(\bm{x}))-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(g(\bm{x}))-f^{*}_{k}(g(\bm{x}))\right|+L_{1}\|g(\bm{x})-\bm{x}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(g(\bm{x}))-f^{*}_{k}(g(\bm{x}))\right|+\frac{1}{2}L_{1}\sqrt{d}\delta_{A}.

(A.72)

Combining (
A.7.3
) and (
A.7.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}}\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\cap G}\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})-\frac{1}{2}L_{1}\sqrt{d}\delta_{A}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}\cap G}\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{A}^{-d}(4+2M_{\beta}^{2})\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime 2}\right),

where the second inequality is because
\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})-\frac{1}{2}L_{1}\sqrt{d}\delta_{A}\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})-\frac{1}{2}L_{1}\sqrt{d}\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{1+L_{1}\sqrt{d}}=\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})-\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}L_{1}\sqrt{d}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}=\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})}
implied by the fact that
\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}\geq(1+L_{1}\sqrt{d})\delta_{A}
given in Lemma
A.10
. Then taking union bound over all
k\in\mathcal{K}
we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\sup_{\bm{x}\in S_{q,k}^{RS}}\left|\widehat{f}_{q,k}^{RS}(\bm{x})-f^{*}_{k}(\bm{x})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon_{q}^{\prime}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{M_{\beta}}}{\lambda_{0}n_{q,k}h_{q,k}^{d}}C\bigg|\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{q-1}^{RS},\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{q-1}^{RS},N_{q,k}^{RS}=n_{q,k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K\delta_{A}^{-d}(4+2M_{\beta}^{2})\exp\left(-C_{K}n_{q,k}^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}\epsilon_{q}^{\prime 2}\right),

which finishes the proof since
h_{q,k}=n_{q,k}^{-1/(2\beta+d)}
.
\square

Appendix B
Experimental Details

B.1
Algorithm Inputs

Due to the absence of historical data for parameter tuning in our simulated realistic environment, all algorithm configurations were set using one of two approaches: ad hoc choices or the hyperparameters reported in their original publications. Specific values are enumerated below. This practice is standard in online learning experiments where prior tuning is infeasible, which has also been applied by
8
;
20
.

B.1.1
Synthetic Dataset

The design parameters for linear contextual bandit algorithms are configured as follows:

•

For the Fair OLS Bandit algorithm, we set
C_{a}=20
,
C_{b}=1
, and
h=1.2
.

•

For the Robust Fair OLS Bandit algorithm, we set
\gamma_{\text{lin}}=4
, and
\kappa=2
, with other parameters same as the Fair OLS Bandit algorithm.

•

For the OLS Bandit algorithm, we use
q=2
and
h=1.2
. These parameters were chosen to provide a moderate forced sampling rate while maintaining a suitable margin for this problem instance.

•

For the UCB Bandit algorithm, the confidence bound for the mean reward of arm
k
at time
t
is computed as:

\text{UCB}_{k}(t)=\widehat{\mu}_{k}(t)+\sqrt{x_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}(0.01I+\sum_{s\in N_{k}(t)}x_{s}x_{s}^{\mathrm{T}})^{-1}x_{t}}\left(0.05\sqrt{d\log\left(T(1+4t/0.01)\right)}+0.2\right),

where
\widehat{\mu}_{k}(t)
is the estimated mean reward,
I
is the identity matrix, and
N_{k}(t)
is the index of times arm
k
has been pulled up to time
t
. The hyperparameters are derived from theoretical analysis under the assumption that problem parameters are known.

The design parameters for smooth contextual bandit algorithms are configured as follows:

•

For the Fair Smooth Bandit algorithm, we use
|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|=\left\lceil 0.2\times 4^{q\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}+\log T\right\rceil
.

•

For the Robust Fair Smooth Bandit algorithm, we use

|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|^{RS}=\left\lceil 0.2\times\left(0.03\times C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}+\log T\right\rceil

and
\epsilon_{q}^{RS}=(2^{-q}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\vee T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}+0.3\times C|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}
.

•

For the Simplified Smooth Bandit algorithm, we set
c_{1}=0.5
,
c_{2}=1
, as recommended.

B.1.2
Real-world Dataset

The design parameters for linear contextual bandit algorithms are configured as follows:

•

For the Fair OLS Bandit algorithm, we set
C_{a}=50
,
C_{b}=5
, and
h=0.8
.

•

For the Robust Fair OLS Bandit algorithm, we set
\gamma_{\text{lin}}=2
, and
\kappa=0.01
, with other parameters same as the Fair OLS Bandit algorithm.

•

For the OLS Bandit algorithm, we use
q=2
and
h=0.8
. These parameters were chosen to provide a moderate forced sampling rate while maintaining a suitable margin for this problem instance.

•

For the UCB Bandit algorithm, the confidence bound for the mean reward of arm
k
at time
t
is computed as:

\text{UCB}_{k}(t)=\widehat{\mu}_{k}(t)+\sqrt{x_{t}^{\mathrm{T}}(0.01I+\sum_{s\in N_{k}(t)}x_{s}x_{s}^{\mathrm{T}})^{-1}x_{t}}\left(0.05\sqrt{d\log\left(T(1+4t/0.01)\right)}+0.2\right),

where
\widehat{\mu}_{k}(t)
is the estimated mean reward,
I
is the identity matrix, and
N_{k}(t)
is the index of times arm
k
has been pulled up to time
t
. The hyperparameters are kept the same with synthetic dataset.

The design parameters for smooth contextual bandit algorithms are configured as follows:

•

For the Fair Smooth Bandit algorithm, we use
|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|=\left\lceil 0.15\times 4^{q\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}+\log T\right\rceil
.

•

For the Robust Fair Smooth Bandit algorithm, we use

|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}|^{RS}=\left\lceil 0.15\times\left(0.008\times C^{\frac{2\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}}\vee 4^{q}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{2\beta}}\left(\log T\right)^{\frac{2\beta+d}{\beta^{\prime}-1}}+\log T\right\rceil

and
\epsilon_{q}^{RS}=(2^{-q}\wedge C^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2\beta^{\prime}-1}})(\log T)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}-1-2\beta}{2\beta^{\prime}-2}}\vee T^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}+0.05\times C|{\mathcal{T}}_{q}^{RS}|^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}
.

•

For the Simplified Smooth Bandit algorithm, we set
c_{1}=0.5
,
c_{2}=1
, as recommended.
